Order 8 applies: where a class cannot be closed without new surface, it is named and pinned, not half-fixed.)** **The threat this ticket answers (owner order 11), in two sentences:** a known value written with other characters ADDED between or on its letters and digits (zero-width or other invisible characters, marks, line breaks, spaces, punctuation), or with look-alike letters, other widths or other case, must not reach a model; that is closed by the look-alike check (`scan_keyset` drops those characters and P2d-2 refuses the text), not by `has_hidden_channel`. The look-alike check catches a known value of at least 12 characters with a scan key of at least 8 letters and digits (P2d-2's length rule; for stored NAME and ADDRESS values only if precondition P-1 shows at most 1 refusal in 200 benign texts, lead order 13(1)) whose own letters and digits are still in the text, with other characters added between or on them (zero-width or other invisible characters, marks, modifier letters such as the Arabic tatweel, line breaks, spaces, punctuation), or with look-alike letters that the table lists, other widths or other case; a copy written with percent escapes, HTML entities, or JSON or backslash escapes is read through `runmap.undo` (design rows LK-PCT, LK-ENT, LK-ENT-VAL). It does NOT catch, in v1: a shorter known value, or one whose scan key has fewer than 8 letters and digits (for example 12 characters of which 5 are punctuation), written in any way but plainly (look-alike, other-case, marked and split copies of secrets of 8 to 11 characters, which are common passwords, reach the model; the redactor masks every plain copy of a secret kind of 4 or more characters; of a secret kind of exactly 3 characters, and of every other kind, it masks only a plain copy of 3 or more characters that stands alone, is not all digits under 6 and is not a common word); any variant copy of a stored NAME or ADDRESS value if P-1 shows more than 1 refusal in 200 texts; a digit written as a letter where the table has no entry for it (no entry gives `0` or `1`, so ASCII `O` and Cyrillic or Greek `о` or `ο` for `0`, and ASCII `l` for `1`, are not folded; Cyrillic Ze for `3` is); a letter written as a digit (for example `0` for `o`, `1` for `l`, `5` for `s`, `6` for `b`; only `3` for `z` is found, by the class map); a letter or digit the table lacks (for example Cyrillic `и`, and enclosed forms with no compatibility decomposition and no table entry, for example negative circled or negative squared Latin letters, regional-indicator letters, and negative or double circled digits and numbers; row PIN-ENCL selects them by their Unicode names); one copy that needs the capital reading (pass A) at one place and the lower-case reading (pass B) at another, in one word or across words (for example `ΗΗ` against `hn`), or a copy that both replaces a letter by a raised modifier letter and inserts another raised modifier letter (the t view reads the first as missing, the undo view reads the second as a letter; each alone is found); a conjoining Korean jamo placed after a Korean value, or the second part of a two-part vowel sign (category Mc) in 11 scripts (see 'P1b: the DENY keys'); a letter that a font draws as nothing but that is neither one of the seven `FILLERS` nor of a category the fold drops (none is known; FILL-NAMES checks every letter whose Unicode name says FILLER or BLANK); and a known value ENCODED, written as bits or bytes in invisible characters, marks, whitespace or selectors, or in base64 or hex. Neither P1 nor the look-alike check reads such an encoded copy, except an ASCII value written in tag characters, which the tag texts T1 to T3 decode; elsewhere in the core only the tool-output scrubber removes the exact hex and base64 spellings of a secret handed to a tool (`tools.py` `_secret_forms`), and nothing else in v1 (as of `tools.py` `_secret_forms`, `ce70eb5`) is built to catch an encoded copy of a known value. (1) **ADV-R13-1, enclosed and dingbat forms (PENDING LEAD 49; ranges corrected in revision 16: see PIN-ENCL):** the negative circled and squared letters (U+1F150-1F189), the regional-indicator letters (U+1F1E6-1F1FF) and the dingbat circled digits (U+2776-2793, U+24F5-24FF) have no compatibility decomposition and no table entry, so a copy written with them is lost or kept as a non-ASCII character and matches no key. The coverage sentence now names them (smaller surface; about 100 table entries are NOT added in v1; table 677 and 733 unchanged). New P1b row PIN-ENCL pins the limit from `unicodedata`; the v1.1 table gains one row (26 rows). (2) **ADV-R13-2, the mixed raised-letter copy (PENDING LEAD 50):** revision 14 wrote that the raw Lm removal stops a raised letter from first decomposing to a plain one and that U+02B0 is the only code point that the key drop alone cannot find. Both were wrong: the raw removal protects an INSERTED raised letter and breaks a SUBSTITUTED one in the t view (the undo view, whose first step is NFKC, repairs it), and 65 Lm characters decompose under NFKD to an ASCII letter (266 to some letter). A copy that both replaces a letter by a raised letter and inserts another raised letter is found by neither view: it is now stated in the coverage sentence and pinned by R13-LM-MIX (one row, three asserts). A third reading (the reviewer's option (b)) is REJECTED: it does not find the mixed copy either (NFKD sends U+1D43 to `a` and U+02B0 to `h`) and doubles the keys to 16. A new assert in the generated-table test says that no source character of the table is of category Lm. The v1.1 needle-API row also closes the mixed copy. (3) **ADV-R13-3, blank signs (PENDING LEAD 51):** `FILLERS` gains U+13441 and U+13442 (7 characters); the reviewer's `Default_Ignorable_Code_Point` test is not taken (`unicodedata` has no such property; a hand copy would be a second source of truth); the P1a row FILL-NAMES tests by name. Order 8 applies (cut surface wherever deleting a feature closes a finding): four cuts and two smaller removals.** **The threat statement of revision 12 is superseded by the one in the revision 13 paragraph above.** (1) **ADV-R10-1, a cut (PENDING LEAD 38):** the entity step is removed from `scan_keyset`. Cause, verified by the resolve: with the entity step first, a `&` before a legacy entity name (106 names that `html.unescape` decodes without a semicolon, among them `lt`, `copy`, `not`, `amp`) eats the letters of a known value (`html.unescape("&LTQ-VAULT-8812")` is `"<Q-VAULT-8812"`), so a copy's key depended on its neighbours. ## Words used in this ticket (defined once)
- **fold**: the function that turns text into a comparison form in which look-alike characters, invisible characters and case no longer matter. **key**: the folded text (a string) a view returns. A key can hold personal data; never log it. **table_digest**: the SHA-256 of the look-alike table (not of a key).
- **view**: one named function that returns a key or a key-set for one purpose. The two view names are exactly `identity_key` (P1a) and `scan_keyset` (P1b); `id_key`, `line_view` and the constant `VIEWS` are cut to v1.1 (ADV-R8-6, ADV-R8-7) and `id_keyset` was cut earlier (ADV-R7-6); no other spelling is used anywhere (`names_equal` is a prose helper, not a view).
- **DENY decision**: "block this if it looks like a protected value" (blocklists, leak scans, same person); over-matching is the safe side. **ALLOW decision**: "accept only if it IS the verified value"; the fold is never used for it; `allow_equal` is the only allow path.
- **reading**: one complete way of turning a text into a key. A **key-set** is the small set of keys one text gets (one per reading); two texts match when their key-sets share a key. **FOLD_VERSION**: ONE string naming exactly which fold produced a key (P1a).
- **hidden channel**: a set of characters that show nothing (or almost nothing) and can carry text or bits. Exactly (defined once, used everywhere): (a) the tag block U+E0000 to U+E007F; (b) every character with the Unicode property `Variation_Selector` other than U+FE0E and U+FE0F: U+180B to U+180D, U+180F, U+FE00 to U+FE0D, U+E0100 to U+E01EF (the property set minus the two emoji selectors, not a hand-written pair of ranges: round-4 finding A1). **Revision 11 cut the tiers (ADV-R9-4):** other invisible characters (format, control, private-use, lone-surrogate and unassigned characters) are NOT part of this definition; `fold` and the scan keys drop them, and the look-alike check, not `has_hidden_channel`, catches a known value hidden with them. Revision 7's flat "invisible set" and revision 8's two tiers are both gone. The two emoji selectors U+FE0E and U+FE0F are NOT part of this definition either (revision 12 cut hidden-channel rules 3 and 4, PENDING LEAD 40): each carries one bit and is uncounted and unbounded, like whitespace. **What the look-alike check catches and does not catch (one sentence, identical in six places: the paragraph 'What revision 15 changed' at the top, this item, Signals, Stated capacity, Stated limits and PENDING LEAD 39; ADV-R10-2, ADV-R11-2): The look-alike check catches a known value of at least 12 characters with a scan key of at least 8 letters and digits (P2d-2's length rule; for stored NAME and ADDRESS values only if precondition P-1 shows at most 1 refusal in 200 benign texts, lead order 13(1)) whose own letters and digits are still in the text, with other characters added between or on them (zero-width or other invisible characters, marks, modifier letters such as the Arabic tatweel, line breaks, spaces, punctuation), or with look-alike letters that the table lists, other widths or other case; a copy written with percent escapes, HTML entities, or JSON or backslash escapes is read through `runmap.undo` (design rows LK-PCT, LK-ENT, LK-ENT-VAL). It does NOT catch, in v1: a shorter known value, or one whose scan key has fewer than 8 letters and digits (for example 12 characters of which 5 are punctuation), written in any way but plainly (look-alike, other-case, marked and split copies of secrets of 8 to 11 characters, which are common passwords, reach the model; the redactor masks every plain copy of a secret kind of 4 or more characters; of a secret kind of exactly 3 characters, and of every other kind, it masks only a plain copy of 3 or more characters that stands alone, is not all digits under 6 and is not a common word); any variant copy of a stored NAME or ADDRESS value if P-1 shows more than 1 refusal in 200 texts; a digit written as a letter where the table has no entry for it (no entry gives `0` or `1`, so ASCII `O` and Cyrillic or Greek `о` or `ο` for `0`, and ASCII `l` for `1`, are not folded; Cyrillic Ze for `3` is); a letter written as a digit (for example `0` for `o`, `1` for `l`, `5` for `s`, `6` for `b`; only `3` for `z` is found, by the class map); a letter or digit the table lacks (for example Cyrillic `и`, and enclosed forms with no compatibility decomposition and no table entry, for example negative circled or negative squared Latin letters, regional-indicator letters, and negative or double circled digits and numbers; row PIN-ENCL selects them by their Unicode names); one copy that needs the capital reading (pass A) at one place and the lower-case reading (pass B) at another, in one word or across words (for example `ΗΗ` against `hn`), or a copy that both replaces a letter by a raised modifier letter and inserts another raised modifier letter (the t view reads the first as missing, the undo view reads the second as a letter; each alone is found); a conjoining Korean jamo placed after a Korean value, or the second part of a two-part vowel sign (category Mc) in 11 scripts (see 'P1b: the DENY keys'); a letter that a font draws as nothing but that is neither one of the seven `FILLERS` nor of a category the fold drops (none is known; FILL-NAMES checks every letter whose Unicode name says FILLER or BLANK); and a known value ENCODED, written as bits or bytes in invisible characters, marks, whitespace or selectors, or in base64 or hex. Neither P1 nor the look-alike check reads such an encoded copy, except an ASCII value written in tag characters, which the tag texts T1 to T3 decode; elsewhere in the core only the tool-output scrubber removes the exact hex and base64 spellings of a secret handed to a tool (`tools.py` `_secret_forms`), and nothing else in v1 (as of `tools.py` `_secret_forms`, `ce70eb5`) is built to catch an encoded copy of a known value.** **These rules bound the COUNTED channels only (tags and the free selectors of rule 2); every other format, control, private-use, surrogate and unassigned character, combining marks, whitespace characters, U+FE0E and U+FE0F, and characters written as HTML entities are NOT counted and are UNBOUNDED in v1 (ADV-R7-4, ADV-R8-4, ADV-R9-4, ADV-R10-2, ADV-R10-3; the figures are under Signals).** **refuse**: the caller treats the text as blocked.

## The three pull requests, in order
- **P1m**: the PR #50 mutation runner and judge with their tests and the 21 mutants that target them. **P1a**: move the module, FOLD_VERSION, `identity_key`, size rule (and its SEAM-1 text), pin check, notices, hidden-channel signal. **P1b**: gaps 1-5 (gap 7, both halves, is cut to v1.1: no decoder runs in any view), the DENY keys (`scan_keyset` and its contract), `same_person` widened. Order: P1m, P1a, P1b; the look-alike package P2d-2 follows P1b (order 11).
- **Why P1m exists at all (ADV-R4-N3):** it is new surface, accepted only because P1a's and P1b's mutants need a runner and core has none. **P1m OWNS** `tools/mutation_runner.py`, `tools/__init__.py`, `src/hadavin_sdk/testing/mutation.py`, `src/hadavin_sdk/testing/__init__.py`, `_pk_tree` and the 21 runner and judge mutants. The round-5 correctness review (R5-C10) found P2a creating `testing/__init__.py` and `testing/contracts.py` too; P2a now says `testing/contracts.py` is not taken (reported by the round-6 and round-7 correctness reviews; UNVERIFIED by me). The round-6 correctness review (COR-R6-2) reported that P2a revision 8 still created its OWN `_pk_tree`; the round-7 correctness review (COR-R7-3) reports that P2a revision 9 imports `_pk_tree` from P1m and says `testing/contracts.py` is not taken (UNVERIFIED by me: I did not re-read P2a in this revision). **Closed in revision 9 (COR-R7-3).** **Overlap row (Decision 8; relay to P2a below): P1m owns `tools/__init__.py`, `testing/__init__.py`, `testing/mutation.py`, `tools/mutation_runner.py` and `_pk_tree`; P2a creates none of them.** P2a cites this paragraph by line numbers (its line 9 says "lines 16-18"); the cite should name the item "P1m OWNS" instead of lines. **Relay to P2a (closed, COR-R7-3):** P2a imports `_pk_tree` from P1m (`tests/test_mutation_runner.py`) and cites P1 by item name except at its line 114, which says '`P1.md` line 18' (a line number, already stale; P2a must change it to the item name 'P1m create'); relayed in the Couplings, Revision 15 additions (2).
- **What downstream packages wait for** (plan rows still say "P1"; P8b updates them; PENDING LEAD): any DENY decision waits for **P1b**. **P2d-2 FOLLOWS P1b (lead order 11; ADV-R7-1, COR-R7-1).** Its named caller is `check_lookalike` (design `handover/designs/lookalike-leak.md`, section 5), which calls the public `scan_keyset` of P1b; that call is the reason P1b is a dependency of P2d-2. The branches of revision 8 ("if lead item L19 stands, P2d-2 makes no `deny_hit` call and does not wait for P1b") are DELETED: order 11 reverses order 9(2) in part. **P1's own merge chain, the only order this ticket states (Depends, Decisions items 6 and 8; ADV-R11-1): P1m, then P1a, then P1b** (P1b merges right after P1a; its only dependency is P1a). P2d-2 follows P1b AND P2d-1 (order 11; P2d.md lines 3, 30, 33, 34, 264, 266, 315, 334 and 339 still say otherwise; relayed). **P1 does NOT order P2a, P2d-1 or the five P4d pull requests among themselves; their tickets do.** The combined order, derived from the Depends-on lines of P2a, P2d and P4d and recommended to the lead as PENDING LEAD 44: P1m, P1a, P1b; then P3a and the five P4d pull requests in P4d's order (P4d-a1, P4d-a2-i, P4d-a2-ii, P4d-b, P4d-c); then P2a; then P2d-1; then P2d-2. **`defaults.py` order: P1a is the only P1 pull request that edits `defaults.py`, and it goes first; the other editors (P4d-a2-i, P4d-b, P2a, P2d-2 with its `LOOKALIKE_*` constants, P4a-1, P4b2) follow their merge order (PENDING LEAD 44).** **`CHANGELOG.md` order: P1m, P1a, P1b, then the others in merge order (append only).** P2d-2 adds its own `LOOKALIKE_*` constants to `defaults.py` (P2d-2 owns the `LOOKALIKE_*` list; **P1 adds none**). **P5a depends on P1a and P1b** for the fold (it maps `TextnormError` by its base class, see Errors) and on P1m only for the runner that runs its own mutants (P5a line 105; P5a's item 7, line 120, writes "P1a, P1b, P1m"); the two statements agree, because P1m is merged before P1a anyway (R5-C17: nothing to change in either ticket except the P5a plan-row wording the lead owns). **Relay for P5a: written in ONE place, the Relays below (Revision 14 additions (3); COR-R12-N-2).** P6a wires the unidata pin into the client profile (it owns `config.py`). P8a re-pins the demos once, after P1b (see Rollback).
- **What P1 supplies to the look-alike check, and what the design supplies (lead order 11, the owner's table of rows; ADV-R7-1).** The check itself, its three call sites, the exception it raises, the finder (grouping, split, cache) and the handling of issued placeholders are in `handover/designs/lookalike-leak.md` and in P2d-2; P1 supplies the fold only. Row by row: look-alike LETTERS of another script (Cyrillic, Greek, Armenian, Cherokee): the generated table of P1a plus the class maps `z` to `3` and `l` to `i` of P1b; full-width forms, ligatures and combining marks: NFKD (step S1) and the Mn/Me drop; zero-width, soft-hyphen and tag characters: the drop rules of step S2 and the tag texts T0 to T3; upper and lower case: the two readings (pass A, pass B); a value split over lines, spaces or punctuation: the drop of every character that is not a letter or a digit in the scan key (ADV-R9-2). **P1 runs no escape decoder (revision 12, PENDING LEAD 38; its only decoding is the tag texts T1 to T3): HTML entities, percent, JSON and backslash escapes come from the design's `runmap.undo` form** (the design reads each checked text as written AND as `undo(t)`). NOT supplied in v1 (v1.1 rows): decoders of any kind, a leetspeak class (PENDING LEAD 29), plain-ASCII digit look-alikes (`1` for `l` or `i`, `0` for `O`; ADV-R8-9, narrowed by ADV-R9-1: the DENY keys now map `l` to `i`, so `aIIow` matches `allow`; only `squeeze(raw, loose=True)` maps the digits), ANSI stripping. What the check may rely on is the contract SK-1 to SK-6 under "P1b: `scan_keyset`". **Each row of the order 11 table has a NAMED P1 row (COR-R10-7):** R11-CYR, R11-GRK, R11-FW, R11-ZW, R11-MARK, R11-SPLIT, R11-UP and R11-LOW (list under "Other P1b tests"); R11-PCT is the design's, not P1's; the property row NB-P runs every class inside any context.
- **Interim rule for P3b and P2a (stored keys):** P1 v1 offers no way to store a fold key. A package that must keep a person or id for later comparison stores the RAW text under its own access rules and re-folds it with the CURRENT `identity_key` when it compares; or it stores nothing. It never stores a digest of a key, and never stores a key and a version label together. A digest of a name is only a pseudonym (anyone with a list of likely names can recompute it) and it would freeze the fold algorithm, which this ticket wants to stay changeable. P3b, and P2a if it stores operator names (UNVERIFIED, P0e asks), state their number D of human decisions per fold change; with raw text kept, D = 0.

## Template fields in the template's order
All 17 template fields appear below. Fields that differ per pull request say which one; a field with no per-PR difference applies to P1m, P1a and P1b alike.

- **Package id and name:** P1m (mutation runner and judge), P1a (the text-folding module, version, views, size rule), P1b (the fix of gaps 1-5, the DENY keys in `scan_keyset`). **Path class:** security (redaction). **Extended review (no round cap): yes** for all three; Tier A for ALL three, **P1m included**: the runner copies source trees, kills process groups and decides what counts as a real kill in Tier A evidence (PENDING LEAD for the plan row, consistent with order 6 (b), which does not list P1m as Tier B). Stop rule of plan section 4 rule 4: two rounds in a row at the final commit with no blocker and no major, evidence green at that commit; there is no round cap; two clean rounds in a row, then the lead's order `S0 EXIT P1` (order 11(4)). Step S1, milestone v1, consumers: all demos.
- **Depends on (merged first):** P0e (memo 1). P1m: nothing. P1a: P1m (for its mutants). P1b: P1a.
- **Problem in plain words:** core main (`ce70eb5`) has no shared text fold: `git ls-tree -r ce70eb5` lists no `guard/textnorm.py`. Its only helper, `guard/runmap.py canonical()` (lines 229-235), does NFKC and drops invisible "Cf" characters; a Cyrillic a, a combining accent, the filler letter U+3164, Arabic-Indic digits and no-break spaces all stay different from the plain text (probed in an earlier revision). Four demos keep their own copy, and PR #49 and PR #50 add two different `guard/textnorm.py`. P1a puts PR #50's version into core as the one base, adds `identity_key` and stamps the fold with a version; P1b fixes the behaviour gaps memo 1 found (gap 6, the version, is P1a) and gives DENY callers one key-set function, `scan_keyset`. Python builds with different Unicode data give different keys (memo 1 section 7), which is why a key must say which fold made it, and why v1 stores none (Interim rule).
- **Source of the code and what is NOT taken** (all `748849c`):

The invoice-specific steps (bar-like letters, circled digits, Chinese numerals, slash dates) are NOT taken and become `not_taken` corpus rows.

- **Interface (names, argument and return types, errors):**
  - **Errors (P1a, P1b).** `TextnormError(Exception)` is the base; it does NOT derive from `ValueError` or `TypeError` (a deny caller that does `except ValueError:` or `except TypeError:` must not swallow it). Subclasses are merged to the ones a test or a message needs to tell apart (ADV-R4-N2, COR-C11): P1a `TextTooLong` (an input over the size rule), `NotText`, `UnidataMismatch`; P1b `FoldNotConverged` only (`UnresolvedEntities` is cut with the entity step, PENDING LEAD 38). `BadNeedle` is CUT with the needle API (v1.1 row, PENDING LEAD 27). Removed from revision 5: `EmptyKey`, `ScanKeysExceeded`, `TooManyNeedles`, `ScanBudgetExceeded` (never raised in any revision; the deadline it belonged to is cut). **One rule for every public function (ADV-R7-2): the size rule raises `TextTooLong`, a non-`str` argument raises `NotText`, and nothing else is raised by the type and size checks.** **Contract: on any DENY or ALLOW path any `TextnormError` means REFUSE, and so does any other exception.** Over-cap means refuse. **This includes folding a KNOWN value (an original) and not only the checked text (ADV-R8-5): any `TextnormError` from folding a stored value means the check refuses the text. P1 never skips and never refuses by itself; the DENY caller (P2d-2 `check_lookalike`) refuses. A caller must not skip an original that P1 cannot fold: the skip would switch that value's protection off, and an attacker who controls a stored value (for example one over the size rule) could do that on purpose (PENDING LEAD 34).** **A lint enforces it (allowlist, see "P1b: the caller rule and the refusal lint"):** a handler that names `TextnormError` or one of its subclasses is banned in `src/hadavin_sdk/**` outside `guard/textnorm.py`, except in the functions listed in the test constant `REFUSAL_HANDLERS` (ADV-R7-5). Messages hold lengths and constants only, never the input or a key. **For P5a (A18):** map `TextnormError` by its BASE class to one closed diagnostic code; never by message and never through `ValueError`; an unknown exception from textnorm is a refusal, not a leak of its text. No reason enum is added now (cut; v1.1 row).
