Prototype cost of the proxy: translating 262,144 characters took 0.0103 s through a dict and 0.0117 s through a proxy (about 13.6 %, COR-C17; UNVERIFIED on the final code). `_confusables.TABLE_DIGEST` is a constant that is NEW in P1a and that the generator writes (it is not in the `748849c` file: COR-R7-10). **It is checked by a test, not at import (revision 16, ADV-R14-2, PENDING LEAD 52):** the P1a test `test_the_checked_in_table_matches_its_digest` computes the digest of the checked-in `_confusables.TABLE`, serialised exactly as `tbl=` is (see FOLD_VERSION), and requires it to equal `_confusables.TABLE_DIGEST`; it never skips (it needs no regeneration, so it runs on every Unicode version, which the generator-reproduces test cannot do: that test skips when the interpreter's Unicode version differs from the data's), so a hand edit of the generated file fails the test. The import-time comparison of revision 7 (COR-R7-10) is deleted: a failing import ends a pytest run with exit code 2, which the judge reports as ERROR and `allow_crash` cannot make good, so the four re-pointed table mutants (#7, #8, #9, #18, whose killers import `textnorm` at the top of their file) could never end KILLED. `TextnormError` stays the base class of the errors. What is lost, stated plainly: a hand edit of an INSTALLED or vendored copy that never ran the core's tests is no longer refused at import; that is the core-sync check's job, and anyone who can edit the installed table can also edit `textnorm.py`, so the import check was no defence against an attacker, only against a mistake that skipped CI. **Public `fold` and `squeeze` change in P1b by exactly these steps (COR-R8-10; checked by hand against the step list of "P1b: identity, the readings, the keys" and against the `748849c` `fold` and `squeeze` bodies, lines 65 to 86; not run).** `fold` is steps S1 to S6 (plus S7 when `collapse_ws=True` and S8 when `casefold=True`), so it inherits gap 2 (a Cn table entry is applied before the drop, S2), gap 4 (dashes, slashes and quotes, S5), the U+FFFD drop (SK-6, S2) and, when `casefold=True`, gap 1's loop (this is the form `identity_key` gets, since `fold_key is identity_key`). `squeeze` calls `fold(raw)` with `casefold=False` (`748849c` line 83), so it inherits gap 2 and the U+FFFD drop but not gap 1's loop; gap 4 and the U+FFFD drop change no `squeeze` OUTPUT, because its last step removes every character that is not A-Z or 0-9 (hand reasoning; UNVERIFIED by run). Gaps 3 (tag texts) and 5 (the drop of every non-alphanumeric character), the class maps `z` to `3` and `l` to `i`, the pass B ordering and the two-reading key-set act only in `scan_keyset`; no decoder runs in any view (revision 12, PENDING LEAD 38). Relay to the look-alike design: its sentence that P1a keeps `fold` and `squeeze` "with today's behaviour" becomes "with P1b's behaviour", because P2d-2 follows P1b. **`__all__` is one explicit list (ADV-R7-14, rewritten in revision 10 by ADV-R8-7 and COR-R8-2).** P1a: 17 names: `TextnormError`, `TextTooLong`, `NotText`, `UnidataMismatch`, `FOLD_VERSION`, `fold`, `squeeze`, `fold_key`, `identity_key`, `same_person`, `AllowVerdict`, `allow_equal`, `names_equal`, `has_mixed_script`, `has_hidden_channel`, `check_pinned_unidata`, `FILLERS`. P1b adds 2: `FoldNotConverged`, `scan_keyset`; total 19 (counted: 17 + 2 = 19; revision 11 had 18 + 3 = 21, revision 9 had 20 + 4 = 24; revision 12 removes `LOOKALIKES`, which becomes private, and `UnresolvedEntities`, which is cut with the entity step). Everything else is private: `_checked` (the input-rule helper, ADV-R12-N1), `_scan_match` (was `scan_match`; ADV-R7-19), `_LOOKALIKES`, `_canon` and the helpers (`_hidden_tier` and `_HIDDEN_EXCLUDED` were cut with the tiers, revision 11). A test pins the list by equality (`textnorm.__all__ == [...]`: 17 names in P1a, 19 after P1b). **The underscore test checks the names BOUND in `textnorm.py` itself, read from the module's AST (top-level `def`, `class` and assignment targets, not imports): each is in `__all__` or starts with an underscore.** (`__module__` does not work for constants such as `FILLERS`, and an imported name such as `unicodedata` is not bound by a `def`, `class` or assignment; ADV-R8-7.)
  - **FOLD_VERSION (P1a, new).** `"alg=<id>|tbl=<12 hex>:<entry count>|ucd=<UTS #39 version>|ud=<unicodedata.unidata_version>"`, built at import. `alg` is an id bumped by hand (P1a `fold1`, P1b `fold2`). There is no provisional marker: nothing stores a key in v1 (Interim rule). **The `beh` hash of revision 7 is CUT (order 8; ADV-R6-10 a; PENDING LEAD 26).** It existed to detect an edit of a key-changing constant without an `alg` bump; the golden file (below) holds one SHA-256 per view over every code point and over the corpus, so such an edit turns Golden Test 1 red anyway, and no other ticket cites `beh` (grep over `tickets/*.md`, done by the round-6 resolve; UNVERIFIED by me). Cutting it removes `BEHAVIOUR_CONSTANTS`, `_BEH_EXCLUDED`, the introspection test, meta-test (f) and the mutant "`beh` built from an empty tuple". **The rule that replaces it: a change to a constant or map that can change the KEY of an accepted text bumps `alg` by hand in the same pull request. In v1 a key-changing change bumps `alg` by hand; Test 1 shows the change, and the reviewer checks the bump; no test enforces it (Golden Test 2 is cut, PENDING LEAD 41).** Constants that only decide accept or refuse (the size-rule constant and the pass limit) change no key. v1.1 row: `beh` back in the version string, trigger: the first package that stores a key (P1c). `tbl` is the table_digest of the in-memory table, serialised as lines `f"{cp:06X}:{value}\n"` in ascending code point order, UTF-8, plus the entry count. `ucd` is `DATA_VERSION` (`_confusables.py` line 9, re-read: `"18.0.0"`). `DATA_SHA256` (`6ed3ee96`, the sha of the UTS #39 data file) is in the generated header (`_confusables.py:8` at `748849c`, re-read). **The file sha of `_confusables.py` itself (`41740c11`) is NOT in that header (a file cannot hold its own hash); it is in `tests/fixtures/textnorm_manifest.json` (`748849c` line 5, re-read; R5-C11).**
  - **Views (P1a: one; P1b adds one).** `identity_key(raw) -> str` (**`fold_key` becomes an alias of it: `fold_key is identity_key`**, so the demos' `fold_key` and `skeleton` get the fixed behaviour and the 114 non-idempotent code points do not stay in an exported name; in P1a it is the `748849c` function, unchanged). There is no `view(name, raw)` dispatcher, no `is_empty_key` and no `require_key` (round-4 COR-C9, ADV-R4-N1: no defect stood behind them), **no `VIEWS` constant and no `id_key` (both CUT in revision 10, ADV-R8-6 and ADV-R8-7, PENDING LEAD 32):** a grep over `tickets/`, `designs/` and `studio-notes/` finds `id_key` as a word only in this ticket and in amendment A14(3) (which rev 9 already reversed: `id_key` was "NOT for DENY"), and `VIEWS` only in this ticket; the only code that calls an id-style key at `748849c` is `guard/egress_text.py`, and it calls `squeeze(..., loose=True)` directly, so `squeeze` stays public. Both go to v1.1 rows with the trigger "a named caller with its own test". **There is no DENY id view in v1; use `scan_keyset`.** **Empty results are defined (COR-C9):** `identity_key("")` and `identity_key` of invisible-only text return `""`; `scan_keyset` (P1b) returns the set of the NON-EMPTY keys, so invisible-only text gives `frozenset()` (probed earlier: `fold_key("​")` and `squeeze("発票")` both return `""`). An empty key is never a value to store or index; a caller that stores or compares keys checks for the empty key itself (the needle call that refused it is cut). **`scan_keyset(raw) -> frozenset[str]` does not exist in P1a (ADV-R8-7, COR-R8-2):** no P1a caller needs it, P2d-2 follows P1b, and the P1b shim stubs it; the empty key-set rows move to P1b; P1b adds it with the public contract SK-1 to SK-6 below. There is no view for ALLOW and none for display (PR #50 docstring lines 4-14). **There is no `_keyset_encode` in `src/` (order 8 cut; ADV-R6-10 b; PENDING LEAD 26):** the golden test writes its own three-line helper (sorted unique keys, each `f"{len(k)}:{k}"`, concatenated) and its rows live there; v1.1 row: a public key-set encoder, trigger: the first package that stores a key-set.
  - **Input rules (P1a): type, then ONE size rule.** Every public function (`fold`, `squeeze`, `fold_key`, `identity_key`, `same_person`, `allow_equal`, `names_equal`, `has_mixed_script`, `has_hidden_channel` and, in P1b, `scan_keyset`; one rule for all of them, ADV-R7-2; `id_key` and `line_view` are cut) first requires `type(x) is str` or raises `NotText` (**one private helper holds both input rules, ADV-R12-N1: every public function starts with one call `_checked(a)` or `_checked(a, b)`; `_checked(*texts)` holds the only `for text in texts:` loop, the only `type(text) is not str` test and the only size comparison, so each P1a input mutant has a search string that occurs once, as meta-test (g) needs**; **arguments are checked left to right, each by type then size (ADV-R13-N3): `(oversize str, non-str)` raises `TextTooLong` and `(non-str, oversize str)` raises `NotText`**) (a `str` subclass with a lying `__eq__` makes `allow_equal` return EQUAL today, probed; `allow_equal(1, 1)` returns EQUAL; `has_mixed_script(b"abc")` raises `AttributeError`; `fold(123)` already raises `TypeError`, so that alone is no fail-before). **A `StrEnum` member is refused too (A15):** callers convert with `str(member)` or `member.value`; probed on 3.13.18: `type(str(R.A)) is str` is True and `type(R.A) is str` is False; a test pins both the refusal and the conversion. Then the size rule, applied to BOTH arguments of the two-argument functions, before any work: **W(text) = sum over the characters of the text of w(c), refused with `TextTooLong` when W(text) is above `defaults.TEXTNORM_MAX_CHARS`; an ASCII text has W = its length (shortcut, like `runmap._expanded_len`: the ASCII shortcut is `runmap.py` lines 145-146 inside the function at 139-147 of `ce70eb5` (`748849c` has the function at 184-192 with the shortcut at 190-191, ADV-R7-15; reviewer's table, not re-read by me), re-read: COR-C6)**. `w(c)` is the longest string the pipeline makes from the single character c, taken over all stages and all passes (NFKD, the table value, the casefold), computed once per DISTINCT character of the text (no cache across calls). The longest table value is the generated constant `_confusables.MAX_VALUE_LEN` (**new in P1a, written by the generator, COR-R7-10**; the reviewer reports that `guard/egress_text.py` has an unrelated `MAX_VALUE_LEN = 4096`; I did not read that file, and the name is kept because the two live in different modules) (**3 today**: 614 entries have 1 character, 61 have 2, 2 have 3, re-probed in revision 6 on the `748849c` table; the two 3-character values are U+A516 to `lll` and U+1F75C to `sss`; the 56 gap-2 entries have 49 of 1 and 7 of 2, probed earlier from the data file, so P1b keeps 3); a test asserts `MAX_VALUE_LEN == max(len(v) for v in TABLE.values())` (so the old "longest value is 2" claim, which was false, cannot return). **No produced key is checked against the cap (ADV-R10-5 part 3, PENDING LEAD 42):** every step acts per character or only joins (NFKC), and w(c) is the longest output of one character over all stages and passes, so no key can be longer than W(raw); the docstring says so, and the property row `len(k) <= W(raw)` (SK-2) fails if w is ever computed wrong, which is what the cut check guarded.
  - **SEAM-1 (moved here from P2d; one answer, COR-C3).** The weighted rule stays: it is what bounds the real scan cost. The rule the P1a tests pin, in plain words: **a document whose weighted length W is within `TEXTNORM_MAX_CHARS` passes; 262,144 characters U+A516 (each makes `lll`, so W = 786,432) are refused.** Consequence for the redactor (C1, A5): "a document the redactor accepts is never refused by the fold" holds for ASCII text only. The redactor counts the NFKD length of each distinct character (`runmap.py` lines 135-147 at `ce70eb5`); the fold counts more where a table value or the casefold is longer than the NFKD form (worst ratio 3, probed over all code points earlier), so the fold can refuse a non-ASCII text the redactor accepts. **P2d states in one sentence which documents it refuses** (a text of mostly U+A516, U+1F75C and the few other characters whose table value is longer than their NFKD form); refuse is the safe side. The option `TEXTNORM_MAX_CHARS = MAX_REDACT_CHARS * MAX_VALUE_LEN` is dropped (time at that size unmeasured). Tests: 131,072 and 131,073 ASCII characters both pass (the old reading of the rule refused the second), 262,144 and 262,145 ASCII characters (second refused), 87,382 U+A516 (W = 262,146, refused) and 87,381 (accepted), 262,144 U+A516 refused (SEAM-1), `"ﷺ"` x (cap/18 + 1) refused before the work, **and the second argument is checked too (ADV-R10-5): `same_person("a", U+A516 x 87,382)` and `allow_equal(U+A516 x 87,382, "a")` both raise `TextTooLong` (W = 262,146; one row, both orders; **ADV-R12-N1: the row also asserts `names_equal("a", U+A516 x 87,382)` raises `TextTooLong`, so all three two-argument functions are covered**; it kills the mutant "`for text in texts:` replaced by `for text in texts[:1]:`")**, and a text of 128,896 distinct characters (prototype: the per-character step takes 0.144 s).
  - **Pin check (P1a).** `check_pinned_unidata(expected: str | None = None, *, strict: bool = False) -> None` (R5-C8: a default written as `expected=defaults.TEXTNORM_PINNED_UNIDATA` would bind the constant when the function is defined and contradict "constants are read at CALL time"): when `expected` is None the function reads `defaults.TEXTNORM_PINNED_UNIDATA` INSIDE its body, so a test that patches the constant sees the patch; a row patches it and expects the new value to be used (and a mutant that binds it at definition is killed by that row); `strict=True` raises `UnidataMismatch`, otherwise `warnings.warn`. Pilot and prod call it with `strict=True` (author proposal, PENDING LEAD; wiring is P6a's, which owns `config.py`).
  - **Signals (P1a).** `has_hidden_channel(text) -> bool` is True when EITHER of these two rules holds. **Cut in revision 8 (order 8; ADV-R6-10 c; PENDING LEAD 26): `has_bidi_control` and `refuse_bidi`.** No ticket calls them (grep over `tickets/*.md`, round-6 resolve; UNVERIFIED by me) and the display-reversal attack (U+202E before "lapyap" shows "paypal") targets a human reader, not the scan, which compares keys in logical order; Intake keeps its own check (`_bidi_lines`, `invoicecheck.py:1003`, re-read in revision 8). Bidi marks are not counted by any rule (revisions 8 to 10 counted them as tier P characters). v1.1 row: a bidi signal, trigger: a caller asks. **Rules 1 and 2 are as in revision 7.** **Cut in revision 11 (ADV-R9-4, order 8, PENDING LEAD 35): rules 5, 6 and 7 and both tiers.** What they added beyond rules 1 to 4 was a flag for an UNKNOWN payload (a value the redactor does not hold) written as a lazy run of zero-width characters, as a dense share of prose-use format characters, or as two or more control, private-use or unassigned characters. Against a writer who tries they closed nothing that stays closed: one visible character between bits avoids rule 5, rule 6 let about 31 KB through at the size cap, and a payload refused by rule 7 moves to combining marks or whitespace, which are unbounded. **The look-alike check catches a known value of at least 12 characters with a scan key of at least 8 letters and digits (P2d-2's length rule; for stored NAME and ADDRESS values only if precondition P-1 shows at most 1 refusal in 200 benign texts, lead order 13(1)) whose own letters and digits are still in the text, with other characters added between or on them (zero-width or other invisible characters, marks, modifier letters such as the Arabic tatweel, line breaks, spaces, punctuation), or with look-alike letters that the table lists, other widths or other case; a copy written with percent escapes, HTML entities, or JSON or backslash escapes is read through `runmap.undo` (design rows LK-PCT, LK-ENT, LK-ENT-VAL). It does NOT catch, in v1: a shorter known value, or one whose scan key has fewer than 8 letters and digits (for example 12 characters of which 5 are punctuation), written in any way but plainly (look-alike, other-case, marked and split copies of secrets of 8 to 11 characters, which are common passwords, reach the model; the redactor masks every plain copy of a secret kind of 4 or more characters; of a secret kind of exactly 3 characters, and of every other kind, it masks only a plain copy of 3 or more characters that stands alone, is not all digits under 6 and is not a common word); any variant copy of a stored NAME or ADDRESS value if P-1 shows more than 1 refusal in 200 texts; a digit written as a letter where the table has no entry for it (no entry gives `0` or `1`, so ASCII `O` and Cyrillic or Greek `о` or `ο` for `0`, and ASCII `l` for `1`, are not folded; Cyrillic Ze for `3` is); a letter written as a digit (for example `0` for `o`, `1` for `l`, `5` for `s`, `6` for `b`; only `3` for `z` is found, by the class map); a letter or digit the table lacks (for example Cyrillic `и`, and enclosed forms with no compatibility decomposition and no table entry, for example negative circled or negative squared Latin letters, regional-indicator letters, and negative or double circled digits and numbers; row PIN-ENCL selects them by their Unicode names); one copy that needs the capital reading (pass A) at one place and the lower-case reading (pass B) at another, in one word or across words (for example `ΗΗ` against `hn`), or a copy that both replaces a letter by a raised modifier letter and inserts another raised modifier letter (the t view reads the first as missing, the undo view reads the second as a letter; each alone is found); a conjoining Korean jamo placed after a Korean value, or the second part of a two-part vowel sign (category Mc) in 11 scripts (see 'P1b: the DENY keys'); a letter that a font draws as nothing but that is neither one of the seven `FILLERS` nor of a category the fold drops (none is known; FILL-NAMES checks every letter whose Unicode name says FILLER or BLANK); and a known value ENCODED, written as bits or bytes in invisible characters, marks, whitespace or selectors, or in base64 or hex. Neither P1 nor the look-alike check reads such an encoded copy, except an ASCII value written in tag characters, which the tag texts T1 to T3 decode; elsewhere in the core only the tool-output scrubber removes the exact hex and base64 spellings of a secret handed to a tool (`tools.py` `_secret_forms`), and nothing else in v1 (as of `tools.py` `_secret_forms`, `ce70eb5`) is built to catch an encoded copy of a known value.** (`scan_keyset` drops those characters, so a copy with characters ADDED has the same key; a value written AS bits in them has an empty key and nothing to match.) Tag characters are the hidden channel a model is known to read as text, so rule 1 stays whatever they carry. **What is lost, in plain words:** a lazy zero-width or control-character encoding of a value that is NOT in the redactor's map is no longer flagged by P1; P2d-2's withhold hook flags only tags and selectors (v1.1 row: a zero-width or control-character run signal). **Cut in revision 12 (ADV-R10-3, order 8, PENDING LEAD 40; this REVERSES the round-7 decline of ADV-R7-17): rules 3 and 4** (a U+FE0E or U+FE0F that is not allowed; more than `TEXTNORM_MAX_EMOJI_SELECTORS` of them), the constant, the capacity table and its arithmetic, the "special case" sentence in Words and the v1.1 row "restrict the base to emoji-variation-sequences". Why the premise changed: (1) revision 10 states, and revision 11 kept, that combining marks (about 359 KB) and whitespace (about 159 KB) are uncounted and unbounded at the size cap, while rules 3 and 4 bound only the U+FE0E/U+FE0F channel at about 1.8 KB, so against a writer who tries they close nothing; (2) revision 11 cut rules 5 to 7 on exactly this argument, and keeping 3 and 4 was the one exception to it; (3) ADV-R4-A2 and ADV-R5-4 were defects of a CLAIM ("selectors are bounded"); deleting the claim and stating the channel as uncounted closes them, as the round-9 cut closed ADV-R9-5 to R9-7, so order 7(3) (a found defect is never dropped) is met because no defect is left unstated; (4) an encoded copy of a known value is not caught at all (PENDING LEAD 39), so a 1.8 KB bound on one channel protects no known value; (5) the 256-selector byte encoding (U+FE00 to U+FE0D and U+E0100 to U+E01EF) stays refused by rule 2, so the only channel opened is one bit per U+FE0E or U+FE0F (about 32 KB by symbols at the cap), the same class as whitespace. **What is lost, in plain words:** a heart followed by 500 selectors is no longer flagged by P1. The rule that stays: rule 1 (tags) and rule 2 (every `Variation_Selector` other than U+FE0E and U+FE0F). v1.1 row: an emoji-selector rule, trigger "a pilot, review or red-team run shows U+FE0E/U+FE0F used as a channel". Relay to P2d: the withhold hook no longer fires on emoji text. **Public (ADV-R7-6):** `has_hidden_channel` is public and a caller decides; `scan_keyset` does not refuse on it. **`has_hidden_channel` reads the RAW text only; it runs no escape decoder; a caller that checks decoded views calls it on each view (ADV-R8-8; relay to P2d-2).** Two pinned rows (hand-reasoned, UNVERIFIED by run): `has_hidden_channel("&#xE0061;"*20)` FALSE (the text is ASCII) and `has_hidden_channel(html.unescape("&#xE0061;"*20))` TRUE (the decoded text holds tag characters, rule 1). Characters written as entities are therefore one of the channels that are not counted (see Stated capacity):
    1. any code point of the tag block U+E0000 to U+E007F;
    2. any `Variation_Selector` code point other than U+FE0E and U+FE0F: U+180B-180D, U+180F (Mongolian free variation selectors; category Mn, 2 bits each, dropped by `fold`; names and category confirmed in Unicode 15.1.0 with `unicodedata`; U+180E is Cf and is NOT one), U+FE00-FE0D, U+E0100-E01EF. The set is written from the Unicode property list; a test fails if any code point whose name contains "VARIATION SELECTOR" is missing from it, except U+FE0E and U+FE0F.
    **Stated capacity (ADV-R6-5, ADV-R7-4, ADV-R7-12, ADV-R9-4, ADV-R10-2, ADV-R10-3; probe B of round 8, re-run in revision 9 and revision 10 with `python3 -I`, raw output in `handover/evidence/P1-round8-probes.md`; ONE method for every figure: bits of symbols, positions not counted, so every figure is an upper bound by symbols only).** There is no capacity table any more: its one row (selector rules 3 and 4, about 1.8 KB) is cut with the rules. **Counted: tags (refused by rule 1) and the free selectors (refused by rule 2).** **Not counted and UNBOUNDED in v1:** every other format, control, private-use, surrogate and unassigned character (they are dropped by `fold` and by the scan keys, but a run of them is not flagged), combining marks (Mn, Me: 1,998 code points on Unicode 15.1.0, about 11 bits each, up to about 359 KB at the size cap by symbols), whitespace characters (29 code points with `isspace`, about 4.9 bits each, up to about 159 KB at the cap), U+FE0E and U+FE0F (one bit each: 262,144 bits, about 32 KB by symbols at the size cap; revision 12, PENDING LEAD 40), and characters written as HTML entities (`has_hidden_channel` reads raw text, ADV-R8-8). The two counts and the byte figures were re-run in revision 10 (`python3 -I`, 3.13.18, Unicode 15.1.0: 1,998 and 10.96 bits, 29 and 4.86 bits; 359,279 and 159,186 bytes, rounded down; raw output in `handover/evidence/P1-round8-probes.md`, section "Revision 10 re-run"); they assume the whole text is one such character with the size cap as length, and they ignore positions, so they are upper bounds by symbols only. **The look-alike check catches a known value of at least 12 characters with a scan key of at least 8 letters and digits (P2d-2's length rule; for stored NAME and ADDRESS values only if precondition P-1 shows at most 1 refusal in 200 benign texts, lead order 13(1)) whose own letters and digits are still in the text, with other characters added between or on them (zero-width or other invisible characters, marks, modifier letters such as the Arabic tatweel, line breaks, spaces, punctuation), or with look-alike letters that the table lists, other widths or other case; a copy written with percent escapes, HTML entities, or JSON or backslash escapes is read through `runmap.undo` (design rows LK-PCT, LK-ENT, LK-ENT-VAL). It does NOT catch, in v1: a shorter known value, or one whose scan key has fewer than 8 letters and digits (for example 12 characters of which 5 are punctuation), written in any way but plainly (look-alike, other-case, marked and split copies of secrets of 8 to 11 characters, which are common passwords, reach the model; the redactor masks every plain copy of a secret kind of 4 or more characters; of a secret kind of exactly 3 characters, and of every other kind, it masks only a plain copy of 3 or more characters that stands alone, is not all digits under 6 and is not a common word); any variant copy of a stored NAME or ADDRESS value if P-1 shows more than 1 refusal in 200 texts; a digit written as a letter where the table has no entry for it (no entry gives `0` or `1`, so ASCII `O` and Cyrillic or Greek `о` or `ο` for `0`, and ASCII `l` for `1`, are not folded; Cyrillic Ze for `3` is); a letter written as a digit (for example `0` for `o`, `1` for `l`, `5` for `s`, `6` for `b`; only `3` for `z` is found, by the class map); a letter or digit the table lacks (for example Cyrillic `и`, and enclosed forms with no compatibility decomposition and no table entry, for example negative circled or negative squared Latin letters, regional-indicator letters, and negative or double circled digits and numbers; row PIN-ENCL selects them by their Unicode names); one copy that needs the capital reading (pass A) at one place and the lower-case reading (pass B) at another, in one word or across words (for example `ΗΗ` against `hn`), or a copy that both replaces a letter by a raised modifier letter and inserts another raised modifier letter (the t view reads the first as missing, the undo view reads the second as a letter; each alone is found); a conjoining Korean jamo placed after a Korean value, or the second part of a two-part vowel sign (category Mc) in 11 scripts (see 'P1b: the DENY keys'); a letter that a font draws as nothing but that is neither one of the seven `FILLERS` nor of a category the fold drops (none is known; FILL-NAMES checks every letter whose Unicode name says FILLER or BLANK); and a known value ENCODED, written as bits or bytes in invisible characters, marks, whitespace or selectors, or in base64 or hex. Neither P1 nor the look-alike check reads such an encoded copy, except an ASCII value written in tag characters, which the tag texts T1 to T3 decode; elsewhere in the core only the tool-output scrubber removes the exact hex and base64 spellings of a secret handed to a tool (`tools.py` `_secret_forms`), and nothing else in v1 (as of `tools.py` `_secret_forms`, `ce70eb5`) is built to catch an encoded copy of a known value.** **No limit protects a short secret.** A mark-run rule is NOT added (order 8): it would not close the short-secret case (a 12-character secret at 7 bits is about 10.5 bytes), it is new design, and its false refusals (Vietnamese, Indic, stacked diacritics) are unmeasured; a KNOWN value written with marks is already caught because the fold drops Mn and Me. The v1.1 row stays, with the trigger "a pilot or a review shows a mark or whitespace channel used". PENDING LEAD 39 asks the lead to accept the unbounded channels and the not-caught encoded copies in these plain words. **Choices a reader can SEE (upper or lower case, a look-alike letter, a full-width form, a ligature) are not a hidden channel and are out of scope here; the look-alike check of the redactor handles copies of KNOWN values.** **Stated false refusal (safe side; v1.1 row: an accept rule for these selectors, trigger: a real document or client with Japanese or Mongolian text is refused):** an ideographic variation selector (U+E0100 to U+E01EF) after a Japanese name or a Mongolian free selector (rule 2). **This is a stated limit, no new rule (COR-R6-5):** no client with such text exists (made-up data only) and a false refusal is the safe side. The false refusals that tier N and tier P caused (newer unassigned code points, icon-font glyphs, ANSI escape codes, the Braille blank, U+180E, U+034F, U+17B4, U+17B5, long Persian text) are GONE with the cut; the stated-limits list of revision 10 for them is deleted. **Not counted by any rule (stated limits):** Mn and Me (combining marks) are visible and ordinary text carries them (accents, Indic vowel signs), so a bit channel built from combining marks is neither refused nor decoded and is UNBOUNDED in v1 (PENDING LEAD 33; the mark-run rule is a v1.1 row; the corpus row "1,000 combining marks on one letter" pins only that `fold` drops them). The ten whitespace controls and U+2028, U+2029 (and the other Unicode spaces) collapse to spaces and are not hidden: a channel built from them is not detected and is UNBOUNDED in v1 (COR-R6-4; PENDING LEAD 33; v1.1 row). Lone surrogates (Cs) are no longer counted (revision 11); `fold` drops them (probe A: 10,000 x U+D800 gives an empty string).
    Rows for rules 1 and 2 (the rows of rules 5 to 7 were cut in revision 11, 29 in all; the rows of rules 3 and 4 are cut in revision 12, 15 in all): U+180B, U+180F, U+E0100, U+E01EF, U+E0001 and U+E007F are TRUE; a text of 200 colour emoji (each with U+FE0F) is FALSE (it kills the mutant "rule 2 widened to U+FE0E and U+FE0F"); a name with an ideographic selector (U+8FBB then U+E0100) is TRUE by rule 2; U+FE10 and U+E01F0 are FALSE (they are not selectors); heart (U+2764) + U+FE0F is FALSE; heart + U+FE0E is FALSE; heart + U+FE00 (a free selector after a base) is TRUE through rule 2. **Rows removed in revision 12 (15, with rules 3 and 4; listed so that a reader of revision 11 can find them):** a keycap digit FALSE; `"a"` + U+FE0F TRUE; 513 emoji selectors TRUE; e-acute + 513 x U+FE0F; e-acute + 2 x U+FE0F; heart + U+FE0F + U+FE0F; heart + U+FE0F + U+FE0E; U+FE0F at the start; space + U+FE0F; R4-A; R4-B; KC-A; KC-B; KC-C; KC-D. **England, Scotland and Wales flag emoji are refused as text (ADV-R5-3; stated limit, decided: no accept-rule in v1).** The flags are U+1F3F4 + tag letters + U+E007F, and rule 1 refuses the whole tag block. A document that contains such a flag emoji is therefore reported by `has_hidden_channel` (TRUE), and a caller that refuses on the signal refuses it (the safe side; `scan_keyset` itself does not refuse); one row pins it (`"\U0001F3F4\U000E0067\U000E0062\U000E0065\U000E006E\U000E0067\U000E007F"` TRUE) and a row of the v1.1 table ("subdivision flags", trigger: a real document is refused for one) names the larger answer: accept `U+1F3F4` followed by at most 6 tag characters in a-z or 0-9 and then U+E007F, nothing else. I did not add that accept-rule: it is new surface with no defect shown, and tags are the channel rule 1 exists to close. Variation selectors are dropped by `fold` like other marks (category Mn; `fold("a\U000e0100b")` and `fold("a️b")` are `ab`, probed) and are never decoded; the tag characters U+E0001 and U+E007F are category Cf and also dropped (probed). `has_mixed_script`'s script list is a curated prefix list (`748849c` `textnorm.py` lines 136-139) and Tamil or Runic letters beside Latin are neither folded nor flagged (probed): stated in the docstring, pinned by a hostile row. `has_hidden_channel` is public and a caller decides what to do with it (the many-needle call that refused on it by default is cut to v1.1, PENDING LEAD 27).
  - **Generated constants (P1a).** The generator also writes `TABLE_DIGEST` and `MAX_VALUE_LEN` into `_confusables.py`.
  - **P1b: identity, the readings, the keys.** All step orders below are the SAME in code and tests (C2). One **pass** is two orderings of the same steps. Steps: S1 NFKD; S2 per character: a table source in category Cn is replaced by its table value (gap 2), every other character in the drop categories, a filler or U+FFFD is removed, whitespace becomes one space; S3 NFKC; S4 digits to ASCII; S5 dash, slash and quote map (gap 4); S6 table; S7 collapse whitespace (split and join); S8 casefold. **Pass A = S1 S2 S3 S4 S5 S6 S7 S8** (table first, then casefold; each letter is read by its own case: capital Eta reads `h`, small eta reads `n`; this is the order of the `748849c` `fold_key`). **Pass B = S1 S2 S3 S4 S5 S8 S6 S8 S7** (casefold first, then the table, then casefold again; capital Eta becomes eta and reads `n`). Each reading repeats its pass until the output equals the input: **at most 2 changing passes plus 1 confirming pass; `TEXTNORM_MAX_PASSES = 3` counts the confirming pass**, and a text still changing at the third pass raises `FoldNotConverged` (A16). Prototype probe on `748849c` tables over all non-surrogate single code points: no code point needs more than 2 changing passes, 114 need 2 for pass A (U+03A3 Sigma: `σ` then `o`; the reviewer's reading, UNVERIFIED by run) and 213 for pass B; strings of several characters are UNVERIFIED (the fuzz test finds out). **`identity_key(raw)` is reading A, one string, idempotent**; tag characters are dropped in it (as today: `identity_key("alice"+TAG("x")) == identity_key("alice")`); Cyrillic Ze reads `3` as in PR #50 (`fold("з")` is `'3'`, probed; the generator's `SUPPLEMENT` has `0x437: "3"`); U+FFFD is dropped (a security rule: it is a way to hide a character between two letters; the consequence for a caller that wants a placeholder stand-in is RES-1 below, and SK-6 pins the drop). Rows (both directions, both readings): Eta, Nu, Upsilon, palochka, Sigma (see Tests). **Stated over-matches of `identity_key` (ADV-R4-B4, B5; one row each, all on the DENY side):** (1) it drops EVERY mark of category Mn and Me, so か and が, Devanagari and Thai vowel signs and Vietnamese tone marks compare equal (a unique index or a same-approver check on `identity_key` can refuse a legitimate second person: that is the safe side); (2) the squashed scan key (below) equates `Anna Maria` with `Ann Amaria` and `Li Na` with `Lina`; `same_person` is a REFUSE-side test, so separation of duties may refuse a real second approver, which is the safe side. P3b stores raw text and compares it (Interim rule); it never uses `identity_key` equality to PROVE two people are the same, only to refuse.
  - **P1b: same_person.** `same_person(a, b) = identity_key(a) == identity_key(b) or _scan_match(a, b)` (`_scan_match` is PRIVATE since revision 9, ADV-R7-19; `same_person` is the only public comparison of key-sets) **(the first part stays so that `same_person` is never narrower than `identity_key` equality, and so that it is reflexive: `same_person(x, x)` is True through the identity half even when the key-set of x is empty, ADV-R10-8)**. It is never narrower than identity equality; it is narrower than the `748849c` `same_person` ONLY for the gap-2 entries (A9): old `same_person("p"+U+113D4, "p")` is True (the old fold drops the unassigned code point) and the new one is False (it reads `pl`); 56 entries on 15.1.0, plus the entries a newer Python adds. **Differential test:** group every non-surrogate code point by the OLD `fold_key` (an oracle copied into the test from `748849c` lines 93-95 and 65-77) and assert that new `same_person` holds between all members of a group, except a NAMED list of the gap-2 code points; reflexive "for every x within the cap that converges" (`same_person(x, x)` is True including empty and invisible-only text; it raises `TextTooLong` or `FoldNotConverged` otherwise, which is refuse). It is NOT transitive (capital Eta matches `h` under pass A and `n` under pass B, and `h` does not match `n`); never use it for a unique index, dedupe or group-by; use `identity_key` equality there. `allow_equal`'s LOOKALIKE answer uses this widened `same_person` (C8); EQUAL is still `a == b` only. **On purpose (R5-C17):** `allow_equal("Li Na", "Lina")` returns LOOKALIKE, not DIFFERENT, because the squashed scan key removes the space; an ALLOW caller accepts EQUAL only, so a LOOKALIKE answer is a refusal that says "looks like", which is the safe side; one row pins it (Tests, over-match rows).
  - **P1b: the DENY keys (A1: no choice point left in them).** A reading-independent step order, then exactly two readings. For a text: (1) the size rule on the raw text; **no decoder runs (revision 12, PENDING LEAD 38): every step below acts on one character at a time, except NFKC, which only joins, so a copy's key does not depend on its neighbours (NFKC joins across a boundary only where the second character of a canonical composition survives the drops: a Hangul conjoining jamo after a Korean syllable (RES-R10-1), or a two-part vowel sign of category Mc in 11 scripts (Balinese, Kannada, Tamil, Malayalam, Bengali, Oriya, Sinhala, Grantha, Tirhuta, Siddham and Dives Akuru; for example U+0B47 followed by U+0B3E gives U+0B4B; Unicode 15.1.0, counted over every code point by the round-11 resolve with `python3 -I`; not re-run by me); such a text changes what a reader sees, so it is not a copy)**; **(1b) modifier letters (ADV-R12-M1, PENDING LEAD 47):** every character of Unicode category Lm (modifier letter; 397 at Unicode 15.1.0, counted from `unicodedata` by the round-12 resolve and re-counted by the drafting agent with `python3 -I`; for example the Arabic tatweel U+0640, the Japanese long-vowel mark U+30FC and its half-width form U+FF70, and the raised small letters such as U+02B0) is removed from the RAW text, once per text, before step S1 (**corrected in revision 15, ADV-R13-2: this protects the INSERTION direction, a raised letter added to a copy; it does NOT find a raised letter that REPLACES a letter, which the t view reads as missing and which is found only through the undo view, where `runmap.undo` applies NFKC first and turns U+1D43 into `a`; a copy that both replaces one letter by a raised letter and inserts another raised letter matches neither view and is NOT found, a stated limit pinned by R13-LM-MIX (PENDING LEAD 50); U+02B0 is not the only code point that the key drop alone cannot find but one of the 65 Lm characters that NFKD turns into an ASCII letter (266 turn into some letter; counted over every code point by the round-13 resolve with `python3 -I`, predicate: an ASCII alphabetic character inside the NFKD output; not re-counted by me); a third reading would not find the mixed copy either (NFKD sends U+1D43 to `a` and U+02B0 to `h`) and would double the keys to 16; only a matcher that knows the needle at match time could skip a character, which is the v1.1 needle API**); this is per character, so a copy's key still does not depend on its neighbours; (2) **tag texts** (hidden channel in tag form): T0 = the text with every U+E0000-E007F removed; T1 = tag characters U+E0020-E007E decoded IN PLACE to their ASCII letters (the other tag characters removed); T2 = T0 followed by the decoded tag characters in order; T3 = the decoded tag characters alone. Duplicates and empty texts are dropped; a text with no tag character has only T0. **T0 stays (ADV-R9-10, CONTESTED by the round-9 resolve; ADV-R11-N6):** it is the base text when there are no tag characters. `casefold` has no final-sigma context (`'ΑΣ'.casefold()` is `'ασ'`, run on 3.13.18 by the round-11 resolve), and every step is per character except NFKC, which joins only as stated above, so under substring use T0 is a prefix of T2 (hand reasoning; UNVERIFIED by run on P1b code); no change to the bound. (3) for each tag text and each pass (A, B): run the loop (above); apply the **class maps `z` to `3` and `l` to `i`** on both needles and haystacks, AFTER the table and the casefold (Cyrillic Ze reads `3`, and the needle's ASCII `z` and `3` are one class, so a text with a Ze at the place of a `z` and another at the place of a `3` is found: needle `az3x9` and text `a`, Ze, Ze, `x9` both give `a33x9`; **`l` to `i` (ADV-R9-1, PENDING LEAD 36):** the table sends the capital-I look-alikes U+0406, U+0399 and U+04C0 to `I` and the small palochka, Arabic alef, Runic isaz, Lisu I and three symbols to `l`, so the same visible stroke lands in two classes depending on its source; casefold turns `I` into `i`, and the map joins `l` with it, so `a`, U+0406, U+0406, `ow` and `allow` both give `aiiow`; over-matching, for example `bill` equals `biii`, is the safe side; its false-refusal cost is unmeasured, the design's precondition P-1 measures it); then **remove every character for which `str.isalnum()` is False or whose category is Lm** (ADV-R9-2, PENDING LEAD 36; the second test, ADV-R12-M1, catches an Lm that the fold itself produces, for example U+0149 giving U+02BC and `n`: 41 code points at Unicode 15.1.0; the Lm clause is a different string from `isalnum` in the code, so it has its own mutant; this includes every whitespace character, all punctuation and all symbols, so spacing, hidden separators, filler letters and separators such as `_`, `-`, `.`, `#`, `~`, `@`, `|` and U+FFFD can no longer split a value: a needle `bank 12345` and the text `ba`, ZWSP, `nk`, ZWSP, `12345` both give `bank12345`; punctuation look-alikes that the table lacks, such as U+0701, U+0702, U+A60E, U+0589 and U+A789, are dropped like any other punctuation; the spaced reading of revision 4 is gone, it is replaced by this squashed key: a change from memo 1 gap 5 `fold_views(raw) -> (glued, spaced)`, PENDING LEAD). The key therefore holds letters and digits only; the class maps and the drop do not act in `identity_key`; P1b changes `identity_key` only by gaps 1, 2 and 4 and the U+FFFD drop (see "Kept from PR #50 head"). `scan_keyset(raw)` is the set of the non-empty results: **at most 2 readings x 4 tag texts = 8 keys; a text without tag characters has 1 or 2 keys** (1 when the two readings agree, as for all ASCII); **there is no cap constant and no branch that raises on a ninth key (revision 10, ADV-R8-1: 2 readings x 4 tag texts can never exceed 8, so such a branch is unreachable and its mutant cannot be killed); the bound is a property row and a docstring sentence (SK-2).** **Case reading, said plainly (C2, C18):** the two readings are two whole-text readings by ONE consumer (pass A: a reader who sees a capital letter as the capital Latin look-alike; pass B: a pipeline that lowercases first). They are the choice kept from memo 1 section 5 1b ("a set of keys: key before and after case"). A text that needs pass A at one position and pass B at another is not one consumer's reading: it is NOT matched, pinned by the row `ΗΗ` against `hn` (False). Cyrillic Ze is different (one letter, two readings at different positions by the same reader), which is why it became a class map and not a reading. **Empty and reflexive (ADV-R10-8):** the private `_scan_match(a, b)` is True only if the key-sets share a key; two empty key-sets share none (the stand-in keys of `"!!"` and `"??"` are both empty), so `same_person("!!", "??")` is False; hence `_scan_match(x, x)` is True for every `x` whose key-set is non-empty, and `same_person(x, x)` stays True for every `x` that does not raise, including empty and invisible-only text, because `identity_key(x) == identity_key(x)` (both give `""`). It is NOT in `__all__` (ADV-R7-19): the public comparison is `same_person`, and a caller that compares key-sets uses `scan_keyset` and the SK contract. **Known over-match, stated (ADV-R4-A3 narrowed; one row):** because spaces and punctuation are removed, words that run together in a text are joined: the needle `mark` is found in `tomark us` and in `market`, and `Anna_Schmidt` equals `Anna Schmidt` (ADV-R9-2); modifier letters are removed (step 1b), so `コーヒー` and `コヒ` get the same key (DENY side; P-1 measures the refusals; ADV-R12-M1). This is the DENY side; there is no token-boundary scanner in v1 (cut, v1.1 row); a caller that scans for short values applies its own minimum (P2d-2 owns its minimums; the key-length rule counts AFTER the drop, so a value made mostly of punctuation gets a shorter key and an e-mail address loses 2 characters: relay to the design; the per-view minimums of the cut needle API are in the v1.1 detail section).
  - **Cut in revision 10: the ID view and `line_view` (ADV-R8-6; PENDING LEAD 32).** `id_key` (it was `squeeze(entity_step(raw), loose=True)`) and `line_view` (gap 7's line-break half) have no production caller (grep over `tickets/`, `designs/`, `studio-notes/`; the single other hit of `line_view` is a test-oracle sentence in P5a at its line 89, rev 1). They go to v1.1 rows with the trigger "a named caller with its own test". **The entity coverage is cut too (revision 12, PENDING LEAD 38):** `scan_keyset` runs no escape decoder (its only decoding is the tag texts T1 to T3); the gap 7 row below holds the rows that remain. `squeeze(raw, loose=True)` stays public (PR #50 and `egress_text` use it). `id_keyset` (the id DENY key-set with the class map `3` to `2`) and the mixed-needle rule of ADV-R4-B10 stay cut with the needle API (ADV-R7-6, PENDING LEAD 27). There is no DENY id view in v1.
  - **P1b: `scan_keyset`, the public contract SK-1 to SK-6 (ADV-R7-2; replaces the many-needle call of revisions 5 to 8).** The one thing P1b offers a DENY caller is the key-set `scan_keyset(raw) -> frozenset[str]` described above; the caller compares key-sets itself (the oracle for the look-alike design is `any(k in t for k in K for t in scan_keyset(T))`, GOLD-1). The contract is six rows. **SK-1, SK-2, SK-3 and SK-6 are acceptance rows, each with a mutant; SK-4 and SK-5 are evidence rows: they record a time, assert nothing and have no mutant (ADV-R9-8).** The mutants are in the Mutation plan:
    - **SK-1 (one parameter).** `scan_keyset` is in `__all__` and takes EXACTLY ONE parameter; a test reads `inspect.signature(scan_keyset)` and asserts a single parameter named `raw`. Mutant: a `clock=` parameter added.
    - **SK-2 (bounded output and input).** On a seeded hostile set (every tag-character mix, Eta, A516, 1,000 random mixes) the result has at most 8 keys; **the property row holds on a seeded hostile set in which each of the 4 tag texts holds whitespace and a capital Eta, so pass A and pass B differ: `len(scan_keyset(x)) <= 8`; the docstring says "at most 2 readings x 4 tag texts = 8 keys, by construction" and a docstring test pins the sentence;** **and the property row `len(k) <= W(raw)` holds for every key `k` of the same set (ADV-R10-5 part 3, PENDING LEAD 42; hand reasoning, UNVERIFIED by run: it fails if w is ever computed wrong)**; a text over the size rule raises `TextTooLong`; a non-`str` raises `NotText`. Mutant: an over-cap text that does not raise (the mutant "key cap raised to 9" of revision 9 is DELETED: it was equivalent, ADV-R8-1).
    - **SK-3 (over-matching is the safe side).** The existing key-set rows hold, among them needle `az3x9` and text `a`, Ze, Ze, `x9`, which give the same key `a33x9` through the class map `z` to `3`; and `scan_keyset("a"+U+0406+U+0406+"ow-this-value")` shares a key with `scan_keyset("allow-this-value")` through the class map `l` to `i` and the drop (row 1 of ADV-R9-1; hand-traced, UNVERIFIED by run).
    - **SK-4 (no clock; the time is stated, not enforced).** `scan_keyset` has no clock and no deadline. **S_call is the worst time of ONE call at the size rule (up to 8 key builds).** The prototype measured 0.25 s per key build (capital Eta at 262,144 characters), so S_call is about 8 x 0.25 = 2 s by multiplication; that is ARITHMETIC, not a measurement: UNVERIFIED. **The 0.25 s per key figure is from a prototype that had no `category()` call; step 1b and the final drop each add one `category()` call per character; the P1b evidence run prints S_call again; until then the figure is UNVERIFIED (ADV-R13-N5; no new number is invented).** The evidence runner measures S_call on the final code and writes it into `P1b-<n>.md`. **Worst case of `allow_equal` (ADV-R9-11, evidence only):** on an unequal pair at the size rule it makes up to 2 x S_call, about 4 s by multiplication, UNVERIFIED; `allow_equal` keeps its LOOKALIKE answer on the widened `same_person` (changing it would flip the pinned row `allow_equal("Li Na", "Lina")` from LOOKALIKE to DIFFERENT and lose a spoof label). ALLOW values are short identifiers; the worst case is a cost, not a limit. The rule of revisions 7 and 8, "k x (budget + S) <= the caller's allowance", is DELETED: there is no budget.
    - **SK-5 (many small calls).** 1,000 calls on 20-character texts; the time is recorded in the evidence file and no limit is asserted (the number is for the caller's design, not a gate).
    - **SK-6 (which characters are kept and dropped).** The keys hold letters and digits only; `#`, `~`, `@`, `|` and U+FFFD are dropped (ADV-R9-2; revision 10 said the keys keep `#`, `~`, `@` and `|`). Expected from probe A at `748849c` (round 7) for U+FFFD: UNVERIFIED on P1b code; the row runs on the P1b code and states the result. Mutants: "U+FFFD kept" (in `fold`, step S2) and "the drop keeps non-whitespace punctuation". **The killer of "U+FFFD kept" is an `identity_key` row, not a `scan_keyset` row:** `identity_key("a"+U+FFFD+"b") == "ab"` (hand-traced, UNVERIFIED; the `scan_keyset` drop removes U+FFFD either way, so a key-set row could not kill it). The killer of the punctuation mutant is the U+0701 row under Tests.
  - **RES-1: U+FFFD is dropped, so the look-alike design cannot use it as a placeholder stand-in (the round-7 resolve's own finding; PENDING LEAD 31).** The design (`handover/designs/lookalike-leak.md`, read only; I do NOT edit it) replaces an issued placeholder by a stand-in character before the scan; P1b's keys drop U+FFFD (SK-6), so a stand-in U+FFFD would vanish and join the letters on both sides. Options, recommended first: (1) split the text at the placeholders and scan the pieces, no stand-in; (2) a stand-in that the keys KEEP (since revision 11 the keys keep letters and digits only, so no punctuation stand-in survives); (3) keep U+FFFD only where the design inserted it (needs a rule in P1b that tells inserted from foreign: more surface). **Settled by the design's draft 3 (PL-LK-12): none of the three.** Each issued placeholder is replaced by one space and the whole text goes through ONE `scan_keyset` call (whitespace is dropped by the keys, so no stand-in survives or is needed). Reason: a copy of a value can span a placeholder (an attacker writes an e-mail inside the copy), so per-piece scanning would miss it. P1 needs no change beyond SK-6 (U+FFFD dropped) and keeping `squeeze` and `fold` public.
  - **P1b: the caller rule and the refusal lint (ADV-R7-5; allowlist).** **Caller rules (A4, COR-C11):** an ALLOW caller calls `allow_equal` and lets ANY exception, including `NotText`, propagate to its own refusing top level: propagating the exception IS the refusal (the top level is an API boundary that turns an exception into a refusal; it is not the function that calls textnorm). A DENY caller treats any `TextnormError` as REFUSE. **The lint (an AST test over `src/hadavin_sdk/**`, outside `guard/textnorm.py`):** (a) the existing bans stay: a handler of `Exception`, `BaseException`, `ValueError`, `TypeError`, `AttributeError` or a bare `except` around a call of a textnorm name; (b) NEW, as an ALLOWLIST: a handler that names `TextnormError` or one of its subclasses (**the banned names are built at TEST time, ADV-R9-9: every name in `textnorm.__all__` that is a class and a subclass of `TextnormError`, plus `TextnormError` itself, so a sixth subclass is banned without a lint edit; today: `TextTooLong`, `NotText`, `UnidataMismatch`, `FoldNotConverged`**) is banned unless the enclosing function is listed in the test constant `REFUSAL_HANDLERS`. v1 entries (three, ADV-R9-9): `guard/redact.py::check_lookalike`, the P2d-2 withhold function (P2d names it `_withhold`; the exact names are for the P2d author to confirm: UNVERIFIED), and the P5a function that maps `TextnormError` to its closed code (the name is set by the P5a author: UNVERIFIED; P5a line 64 maps the family by walking `TextnormError.__subclasses__()`, so its handler names the base class and needs the entry). The banned shapes cover a tuple (`except (NotText, KeyError):`), a dotted name (`except textnorm.NotText:`), an alias (`import ... as tn`, `from ... import X as Y`) and `contextlib.suppress(...)`. **Nine fixture rows (8 reported, 1 allowed):** (1) `except TextnormError:` reported; (2) `except TextTooLong:` reported; (3) a tuple handler reported; (4) a dotted handler reported; (5) a module alias reported; (6) a name alias reported; (7) `contextlib.suppress(TextnormError)` reported; (8) `except Exception:` around a textnorm call reported; (9) `except TextnormError:` inside a function listed in `REFUSAL_HANDLERS` ALLOWED. **The three lint mutants of revision 9 are DROPPED (ADV-R8-11): they mutate test-helper code, which conflicts with ADV-R4-A4; the nine fixture rows stay and are the pin.** **`REFUSAL_HANDLERS` exempts ban (b) only (COR-R8-9).** Ban (a) has no exemption: a listed function handles `TextnormError` by name; a broad handler (`except Exception`) for the finder must not have a direct textnorm call in its `try` body. The design must arrange `check_lookalike` that way (relay under Relays); no new fixture row is added because row (8) already reports the broad handler. The same function also handles a `TextnormError` raised while folding a stored value (ADV-R8-5): it counts a hit. The lint catches the named shapes and is not a proof: other swallowing patterns are UNVERIFIED. The keyword bans on `refuse_hidden=False` and `clock=` and the call ban on `PreparedNeedles(` of revision 8 are DELETED with the names they guarded.
  - **Cut to v1.1 (ADV-R7-6; PENDING LEAD 27).** The DENY needle API leaves v1: `needle_problem`, `prepare_needles`, `deny_hit`, `PreparedNeedles`, `BadNeedle`, `id_keyset` (with its class map 3 to 2), the private `_scan_contains`, the seven constants `TEXTNORM_MAX_NEEDLES`, `TEXTNORM_MAX_NEEDLE_CHARS`, `TEXTNORM_MIN_NEEDLE_KEY`, `TEXTNORM_MIN_NEEDLE_SCAN`, `TEXTNORM_MIN_NEEDLE_ID`, `TEXTNORM_MIN_NEEDLE_DIGITS` and `TEXTNORM_SCAN_BUDGET_S` (COR-R8-8: counted with `grep -o` over `git show 8d56284:handover/tickets/P1.md`, revision 8, which names exactly these seven `TEXTNORM_` needle names; I found no non-`TEXTNORM_` constant of that API; revision 9 said "nine" and listed six), the parameters `refuse_hidden`, `clock` and `allow_empty`, the 500-needle performance rows, the clock rows, the 10 `needle_problem` rows, the lint rows for them, and their mutants. **v1.1 row "DENY needle API"; trigger: a package ticket with a named production caller and its own test needs a many-needle refusing scan, or the id DENY view of order 5(d) is asked for by Intake or P5a.** The design is in "v1.1 detail: the DENY needle API". The named users of the old design were P2d-2 (which now calls `scan_keyset` through `check_lookalike`) and P5a (whose plan names no cut name, see the downstream bullet).
  - **Constants and time (A3, C5, A14, C21).** Search is linear in the key length (prototype: a 64-character needle in a 262,144-character key of `"a"` took 1.16 ms per search); with no needle API in v1 this matters to the caller only. Key building: capital Eta at 262,144 characters took 0.25 s per key in the prototype (UNVERIFIED on the final code). The builder re-measures on the final code with read-only tables, 3 passes, the real key-sets, on the worst case of this ticket, and may only LOWER a cap or RAISE a minimum. P1b reads `TEXTNORM_MAX_PASSES = 3` (added by P1a) and the size-rule constants; it adds no constant, and no needle constant and no budget constant exists (ADV-R11-1).
  - **P1b: other.** **Gap 7 (both halves) is cut to v1.1 (revision 12, ADV-R10-1, PENDING LEAD 38; the line-break half was already cut with `line_view` in revision 10, PENDING LEAD 32): no decoder runs in any view.** `scan_keyset` and `identity_key` build their keys one character at a time; HTML entities, percent escapes, JSON escapes and backslash escapes come from the design's `runmap.undo(t)` form, read beside the text as written. U+FFFD keeps its drop (a security rule, any source: it is a way to hide a character between two letters; SK-6 pins it; RES-1). **Known limits (ADV-R4-B3, decided: no decoders in P1):** a character written as an HTML entity (`&#97;`), as a JSON escape (a backslash, `u`, then the four hex digits `0061`), as a percent escape (`%61`) or as a backslash-x escape (a backslash, `x`, then `61`) is NOT decoded by any view; each is a row that pins "not found" (rows under gap 7 below), and decoders are a v1.1 row (order 7: cut surface; they would be new design). The example is the letter `a` and not a digit: an escape of an ASCII digit ends with that digit, so under the look-alike check's substring comparison a digit row would be "found" by accident (RES-R10-2).
- **Neutral default / working alternative:** built-in fold on the standard library only (`unicodedata`, `re`, `enum`, `hashlib`, `types`, `warnings`, `collections.abc`; `html` and `html.entities` are no longer imported, because the entity step is cut in revision 12 (my consequence of the cut, not in the resolve); imports of `748849c` `textnorm.py` lines 36-38 and Intake `invoicecheck.py` lines 94-110). No new dependency. Alternative: written outline (plan section 5): ICU fold, trigger "a client language the standard tables mishandle". A vendored `unicodedata` snapshot is v1.1 (P7n, trigger "a client needs two Python builds"). No registry entry.

Revision 6 said this cannot be avoided by argument lists; the round-5 review (R5-C14) points out that moved test code (`test_promoted_controls.py` lines 1957 and 2051, `subprocess.Popen([sys.executable, ...])`) has no `noqa` in `748849c` (confirmed by `grep -n "Popen\|noqa"` on 2026-11-05: line 1957 is `subprocess.Popen([sys.executable, "-c", "import time; time.sleep(600)"])` and line 2051 is the same call inside a string that a child runs; whether ruff accepts line 1957 is UNVERIFIED, ruff was not run). The runner's own calls pass a variable `cmd` (line 137) and a list that contains a function result (line 153), so an inline literal may not be possible for them; the builder finds out. **Decision: the builder FIRST tries an inline literal argument list (a list written in the call, no variable) for the two runner `Popen` lines (137 and 153) and runs `ruff check tools`; the two `noqa: S603` stay as LISTED exceptions only if ruff still fails, and then the PR text pastes the ruff output and the reason for each.** The runner already uses a fixed argument list, no shell, our own interpreter and, for Windows, the full path of `taskkill`. The three `nosec` comments (lines 19, 137, 153: B404, B603, B607) are, as far as I know, LOW severity, which `bandit -ll` does not report; the builder runs `bandit -q -r tools -ll` and deletes each `nosec` the run shows to be unneeded (UNVERIFIED here: bandit not run). The Windows branch of the runner (`os.name == "nt"`, `_taskkill`, lines 100-103, 134, 149-160) stays: it is shipped tool code, test 441 reads `_taskkill()`, and the demos' copies expect these bytes; it is untested on Windows (CI is Linux only).

With these removed, those tests have no `noqa`.

The digit table is built over `sys.maxunicode + 1` (today `range(0x20000)`, `textnorm.py` lines 52-57, so a future decimal digit above U+1FFFF would stay unmapped; re-probed in revision 6 on Python 3.13.18: both ranges find the same **670 decimal-digit code points that are not ASCII 0-9** (680 decimal digits in all); building over the full range took 0.07 s at import against 0.008 s in the earlier probe); the completeness test stays. **A mutant that cuts the range back to `0x20000` is EQUIVALENT on Unicode 15.1.0 (both ranges find the same 670 digits), so it cannot be killed by any test and it is CUT from the plan (R5-C4; see the Mutation plan arithmetic). I add no test for the range parameter: no defect stands behind it.** If a future Python adds a decimal digit above U+1FFFF, the completeness test (which scans `range(sys.maxunicode + 1)`) fails and shows it. Time at the cap, `748849c` fold, Python 3.13.18 (earlier probe, UNVERIFIED on the final code): 262,144 characters of Cyrillic confusables 0.08 s, ASCII 0.038 s, NFD accents 0.036 s, zero-width split 0.033 s; 262,144 U+FDFA characters took 1.66 s WITHOUT the expansion check, but the check refuses that text (18 characters each), so the worst admitted U+FDFA text is 14,563 characters at 0.093 s (the same number as `defaults.py` lines 34-37 at `ce70eb5`). Peak memory per call at the cap is about ten intermediate strings, roughly 10 MB. **No cache** (author proposal, PENDING LEAD; it replaces the "bounded cache" of amendment A2 item 5): the fold costs 0.04-0.08 s at the cap, no measured need exists, and a cache keeps raw personal text in memory; the dealdesk wrapper keeps its own.
- **Existing core code reused (checked):** `guard/runmap.py` (at `ce70eb5`: `canonical` 229-235, `nfkc`, `fits_after_nfkc`, `_expanded_len` 139-147; at `748849c` the reviewer's table gives `_expanded_len` 184-192 and `canonical` 274, not re-read by me: the size rule is modelled on it, not replaced; `REDACT_BUDGET_S` checked with `time.monotonic()` at lines 449 and 462 of `ce70eb5` (re-read) was the model for the scan deadline of the needle API, which is cut to v1.1, so v1 has NO scan deadline; PR #50 `748849c` measures the same budget with `time.thread_time()` at `runmap.py:484`: recorded in the v1.1 detail section; every line cite here names its head, ADV-R7-15); `guard/redact.py` (1,254 lines) and `k1b/redaction.py` (195) call no fold; `guard/injection.py` (101) squashes to letters only (consumer question for P5a/P5c); `guard/egress.py` line 16 normalises hosts only; `hashing.py` (34) hashes file sets only, so `hashlib` is used directly; `hypothesis` is not a dependency (`tests/demo_askdata/fuzzgen.py` line 1: "no `hypothesis`"): property and fuzz tests build their inputs with **integer arithmetic** (a fixed 64-bit linear congruential step, `x = (x * A + C) % 2**64` with two 64-bit constants A and C that the builder writes in the test file), not `random.Random`, whose method output can differ between Python versions (A11) and which ruff S311 flags; the tests take the HIGH bits of that state (`x >> 33`), never `x % n` of the state (ADV-R5-15). **Checked and NOT relevant (ADV-R5-13):** `k1b/atomic.py` (16 lines), `stats.py` (34), `evals.py` (141), `store/memory.py` (282) and the 23 files of `tests/contracts/` at `ce70eb5` contain no text fold, normalisation or case folding (`git show ce70eb5:<path> | grep -ciE 'unicodedata|casefold|nfkc|normaliz|textnorm'` gave 0 for each, run on 2026-11-05); they are not touched and need no change.
- **Security and least-privilege notes:** pure functions: no file, network, environment or database access at run time; no identity, no read or write right, no secret; the generator is a developer tool outside the wheel. The P1m runner is a developer tool: it starts pytest processes on COPIES of a tree, gives the child an allowlisted environment only (`CHILD_ENV`, `tools/mutation_runner.py:84`; no database address, token or other secret of the caller) and refuses paths that leave the tree (tests 1545, 1626). Personal data: no logging; no exception message, `repr`, `__cause__` or `__context__` holds the input or a key (canary test through every public function). All loops and sizes are bounded by `defaults.py` numbers and every bound fails closed. **Nothing in v1 stores a key or a digest of one (Interim rule).** Plan rule 9 f, g (identity, rights, dev-only flag): textnorm declares `identity="none"`, `rights=()` when P6b2's contract test exists (UNVERIFIED at `ce70eb5`). No real client data in any fixture; every personal-looking value in a fixture is made up.

The proof is the **mutants of the runner and judge itself: 21 entries of `748849c` `tests/promoted_mutants.py`**, counted by their target (`MU` = `testing/mutation.py`, `MR` = `tools/mutation_runner.py`); **every line number in this list is the line of the mutant's NAME string in `748849c` `tests/promoted_mutants.py`, re-derived by `grep -n` in revision 7 (revision 6 gave the next line for all but the first runner entry; R5-C16 convention)**: judge (name lines 604, 611, 618): "an exception line counts as a kill", "crash markers ignored", "short expect accepted"; runner main list: "crash counts as good" (625), "an absolute path is accepted" (918), "a dotdot part is accepted" (925), "a link out of the tree is accepted" (932), "the mutant file is not checked again" (939), "a short expect is accepted" (946), "a timeout is reported as an exit code" (953), "a dash path is an option" (960), "the mutant file is joined as a path" (1118), "a link out of the tree is listed" (1125), "a timeout leaves the grandchildren running" (1167), "a failing tree kill is not caught" (1174), "the baseline timeout is ignored" (1340); the fix batches: "fix: junit with a DOCTYPE is parsed" (1548, killer batch 343), "fix: the runner drops pinned" (1555, killer batch 351), "b2: a refused junit stops the run" (1753, killer batch2 428), "b2: the junit has no size ceiling" (1761, killer batch2 454), "b3: a crash row is good whatever the exception" (2113, killer batch3 401). That is 3 + 13 + 5 = 21 (counted again in revision 6 by the target constants; revision 5 said "16, UNVERIFIED"). They go into `tests/mutation_runner_mutants.py`, each re-pointed to a moved test, run through the runner itself. **Verdicts (COR-C7, COR-R13-1): every one ends KILLED except two that end CRASH with a declared exception: "a failing tree kill is not caught" (`allow_crash=True`, `crash_type="FileNotFoundError"`, snapshot line 1179-1181) and "b2: a refused junit stops the run" (`crash_type="ValueError"`, snapshot 1753-1759; the builder at 1571-1583 sets `allow_crash=bool(crash), crash_type=crash`). The moved mutants file must carry `allow_crash=True, crash_type=<that class>` for each of the two; a CRASH of any other class is not good (`tools/mutation_runner.py` line 290: `crash_ok = v is Verdict.CRASH and m.allow_crash and m.crash_type in first`). The crash fields are read from the BUILT `Mutant` objects, not by grep (a tuple-built batch carries the crash type as its sixth tuple field, snapshot 1758, builder 1571-1583, which a grep for `allow_crash` does not find): the round-14 resolve imported the `748849c` mutant list and read all 21 by name: exactly 2 carry `allow_crash=True` (the two named above) and 19 carry `allow_crash=False`. The P1m builder repeats that read on the moved file (`tests/mutation_runner_mutants.py`) and prints the 21 rows; it never writes "all KILLED" without that print.** "runner: crash counts as good" works only through the override dict `_RETARGET` of `promoted_mutants.py` (starts at line 1800, ends 1885; its entry at 1812 replaces the stale search string of the main-list entry at 625 with the real source line `crash_ok = v is Verdict.CRASH and m.allow_crash and m.crash_type in first`, killer batch3 401); P1m carries the override rows (or rewrites the strings in its own list), because the stale entry matches nothing in the runner. **BOTH overrides are named (R5-C7; rev 6 named only the first):** (1) `_RETARGET["runner: crash counts as good"]` at `promoted_mutants.py:1812` replaces the search string of the main-list entry at 625 (`v is Verdict.KILLED or (v is Verdict.CRASH and m.allow_crash)`, which occurs 0 times in the runner); (2) `_RETARGET["b2: a refused junit stops the run"]` at line 1873 replaces the search string of the entry at 1753 (`except ValueError:  # a refused JUnit`, 0 occurrences) by `except JUNIT_REFUSED:` (the runner has that line at `tools/mutation_runner.py:278`, and `JUNIT_REFUSED` is defined at 94). Classes: zero-width, bidi controls, tag characters (inside a word, adjacent to a needle, the whole text), variation selectors (FE00-FE0F, Mongolian, E0100-E01EF), emoji sequences and keycaps, full-width, Cyrillic/Greek/Armenian/Cherokee confusables, combining marks (1,000 on one letter), filler letters, NFKC expansion (U+FDFA, U+FB03), other-script digits, Unicode whitespace, private use (an icon-font line with 5 glyphs), unassigned code points (8 in a row), a Japanese name with an ideographic variation selector (a stated limit, refused by rule 2), controls, lone surrogates (`"a\ud800b"` folds to `ab`, probed), mixed scripts, an unlisted script (Tamil, Runic), cap+1, empty, whitespace-only.   - **The run:** The full mutation run happens once per PR on the final head, alone, on Python 3.13 (`review-loop-speed-rules`: delta runs for re-pointed and changed mutants plus a 60-mutant sample while iterating). A mutant that SURVIVES is a failure of the plan: the author adds the missing killer or deletes the mutant with a written reason.
- **Evidence commands (exact), and where the raw output is saved:** a separate evidence runner agent (brief starts "EVIDENCE RUNNER"; it runs and saves, never judges) writes raw output under `handover/evidence/P1m-<n>.md`, `P1a-<n>.md`, `P1b-<n>.md` (each file starts with the head sha of the pull request; it must equal the snapshot sha given to the reviewers). **Per-PR file lists (ADV-R4-B9) replace "src tests demos":**

The commands that did run are read-only reads, listed under "Checked in revision 11"; no probe script was run; every new row, every hand trace and every count is UNVERIFIED until the evidence run.
- **Reviewers:** correctness and adversarial, fresh `egm:*` agents, parallel, one snapshot per pull request (`snapshot-before-review`: export the changed files of the head to a snapshot folder; give the exact head sha). They get the diff, this ticket and the evidence only; they never get the author's verdict. P1m is reviewed first and alone; P1a and P1b get their own rounds. Round numbers are stated in every brief; Tier A: no round cap, stop rule above.
- **Done =** per pull request: the Tier A stop rule above (two rounds in a row at the final commit with no blocker and no major, evidence green at that commit) and all gates green: tests, `ruff check`, `ruff format --check`, `bandit -ll` (with `tools` once the lead has added it), repo guard tests, CodeQL, SonarCloud, CodeRabbit. `egm:egm-drift-report` before saying done. The blocker ledger is updated in the same turn as each review.
- **Rollback:** tags `pre-P1m`, `pre-P1a`, `pre-P1b`, created by the lead only. **P1m:** additive (new files, tests, one CHANGELOG line); revertible by `git revert`; the P1m files are pinned by `mutation_manifest.json`, and a demo that copied them must be re-pinned. **P1a:** additive (a new module nobody imports yet); revertible; `defaults.py` and `.gitattributes` changes revert with it. **P1b:** the behaviour change of the keys. It is NON-REVERTIBLE once a consumer has been built against the new keys; there is no stored key in v1, so a revert changes only decisions in a caller that has already merged; the loud-failure test is golden Test 1 (a changed key without a golden-file update fails the build; the `alg` bump is checked by the reviewer, no test enforces it, COR-R11-4) and the P5a mapping of `TextnormError`. **P8a re-pins the demos once, after P1b** (re-pinning after P1a alone would copy keys that P1b changes).

## Not in v1: P1c, stored keys (outline only; lead order 7(3))
Ten lines, no interface, no tests, no mutants, no errors in this ticket.
1. Purpose: let a package STORE a fold key and compare it later across a fold change.
2. Why not now: nothing in v1 stores a key; the Interim rule (store raw text, re-fold with the current `identity_key`) costs nothing; a stored key freezes the algorithm.
3. Trigger: the first package that must store a fold key because raw text may not be kept (P3b; P2a if it stores names).
4. It would need: a stored form that carries the fold version, a parse that refuses a different version loudly, and a salted keyed digest for personal values (never a bare digest).
5. It would need a decision on who migrates stored keys when `alg` changes and who owns that cost (the number D above).
6. Not built: `view_version`, `parse_stored_form`, a `p` marker in the version string, stored-form errors.
7. The version string is `alg|tbl|ucd|ud` since revision 8 (the `beh` field was cut; its v1.1 row names P1c as the trigger that brings it back, because a stored key must be able to tell that a constant changed) with no field reserved for the stored-form marker; adding a field later changes `FOLD_VERSION` once, on purpose.
8. Review class would be Tier A (redaction and personal data).
9. Estimated size: a smaller pull request than P1b; not estimated further.
10. Owner of the decision to start it: the lead, when the trigger fires.

## v1.1 table (revision 16; each row has its trigger; 27 rows: 26 in revision 15, plus the row for blank-drawn letters beyond `FILLERS`, order 16; the enclosed-forms row is rewritten)
| Item | Trigger |
|---|---|
| P1c stored keys (above) | the first package that must STORE a fold key (P3b, or P2a if it stores names) |
| Reason enum for P5a (why a text was refused) | P5a needs more than "refused" in its audit row |
| Decoders (HTML entities, JSON, percent and backslash-x escapes) in the scan keys; meanwhile the look-alike check reads `runmap.undo(t)` (the entity step was cut from `scan_keyset` in revision 12, ADV-R10-1, PENDING LEAD 38; merged with the row "Percent and JSON decoders for the look-alike check", COR-N4) | a consumer reads text from a source that escapes this way and a corpus shows a miss |
| Token-boundary scanner (word edges) | the measured false-refusal rate of the substring scan is above the owner's limit |
| A zero-width or control-character run signal (rules 5 to 7 and both tiers of `has_hidden_channel`, cut in revision 11, ADV-R9-4, PENDING LEAD 35; the 27-member tier P list, tier N, `_hidden_tier`, `_HIDDEN_EXCLUDED`, the enumeration test and the constants `TEXTNORM_MAX_INVISIBLE_RUN`, `_MAX_INVISIBLE_TOTAL`, `_MAX_INVISIBLE_PERCENT`, `_MAX_NOPROSE` come back only with it; it also absorbs the old rows "script-aware rule for tier P", "precomputed set of hidden characters" and "stated false refusals of tier N") | a pilot or a review shows an unknown payload carried that way |
| DENY needle API (cut in revision 9, ADV-R7-6, PENDING LEAD 27): `prepare_needles`, `deny_hit`, `needle_problem`, `PreparedNeedles`, `BadNeedle`, `id_keyset` with the class map 3 to 2, the private `_scan_contains`, the shared deadline across several calls, the per-view minimums, and their seven `TEXTNORM_` constants (design in "v1.1 detail: the DENY needle API"); it also closes the mixed Lm copy (ADV-R13-2), because a matcher that has the needle at match time may skip a character | a package ticket with a named production caller and its own test needs a many-needle refusing scan, or the id DENY view of order 5(d) is asked for by Intake or P5a |
