## v1.1 detail: the DENY needle API (cut from v1 in revision 9, ADV-R7-6; PENDING LEAD 27)
This section keeps the design of revisions 5 to 8 so that a later package can start from it. **Nothing here is in v1: no code, no test, no mutant and no constant of it is part of P1.** It was cut because no package ticket had a named production caller with its own test (P2d-2 calls `scan_keyset`; P5a names none of these functions), and because the surface (6 public names, 7 `TEXTNORM_` constants, 3 parameters) carried most of the review findings of rounds 5 to 7.
- **Functions (as designed):** `prepare_needles(needles_raw, *, view="scan_keyset", allow_empty=False) -> PreparedNeedles` at load time (the WHOLE list fails with `BadNeedle` on the first bad entry; an empty list is bad unless `allow_empty=True`); `needle_problem(raw, *, view) -> str | None` (the same checks on one entry; reason names `not_text`, `too_long`, `hidden`, `empty`, `too_short`, `too_short_digits`, `unfoldable`); `deny_hit(prepared, haystack_raw, *, refuse_hidden=True, clock=time.thread_time) -> bool` (True means REFUSE: on a match, on a hidden channel, on a `prepared` of the wrong type, on a passed deadline, on any `Exception`); `id_keyset(raw)` (per tag text T0 to T3, `squeeze(T, loose=True)` then the class map `3` to `2`, at most 4 keys); the private `_scan_contains`.
- **Order of the checks on one entry:** type and raw cap; the bounded entity step (so `&#xE0061;` becomes a tag character before the next checks); length cap again and `has_hidden_channel`; the key (empty is bad); the minimums on the FINAL key (after the entity step, the fold and the maps, never on the raw entry).
- **Per-view minimums (proposal):** scan view 6, id view 8, digit-only keys 6 (`TEXTNORM_MIN_NEEDLE_SCAN`, `TEXTNORM_MIN_NEEDLE_ID`, `TEXTNORM_MIN_NEEDLE_DIGITS`). **The digit minimum 6 is DECIDED (lead order 8(2)).** The digit rule changes nothing in the scan view (6 against 6) and is weaker than the per-view rule in the id view (6 against 8). Short names are compared by the caller whole-token with `same_person`.
- **Why the digit minimum (the chance-hit paragraph, moved here whole from revision 8):** Why a minimum at all: a short needle in a squashed haystack over-refuses. Prototype (revision 6, 64-bit integer generator, random digits, NOT a measurement of the final code and NOT re-run by the round-5 reviewers; the evidence runner re-measures it): a 6-digit needle was found in 17 of 400 trials in 50,000 random digits (**about 4 %**, 4.3 %; chance expected 1 - (1 - 10^-6)^50,000 = 4.9 %) and in 41 of 200 trials in 250,000 random digits (**about 20 %**, 20.5 %; chance expected 22 %). **Correction (ADV-R5-5, R5-C6): revision 6 wrote "about 8 %" for the 250,000-digit case, which understated the false-hit rate at that size by a factor of about 2.5 (8 against 20.5), and it was copied into the lead's decision text; the figures above replace every copy.** Revision 6 also wrote "expected 5 %" and "expected 25 %", a linear estimate; the exact chance is 4.9 % and 22 %. An 8-digit needle was found in 0 of 400 and 0 of 200 (chance 0.05 % and 0.25 %); a 4-digit needle in 99 of 100. So a 6-digit minimum for digit-only needles still over-refuses numeric tables: 500 such needles against a 250,000-digit table would refuse it almost surely (1 - 0.78^500). **Per needle (ADV-R6-11):** every chance figure above is for ONE needle; for N needles the chance of at least one hit is 1 - (1 - p)^N, so 500 needles of 6 digits against 250,000 random digits is almost sure. **Two positions, both written (Decision 5):** the round-5 resolve keeps 6 (a false hit refuses a document and a person looks: the safe direction); I recommended 8 in revision 6. I now state my disagreement plainly: with a minimum of 8 a 6-digit identifier in a blocklist makes `prepare_needles` fail LOUDLY at load time (the whole list, never a partial list), it is not silently unprotected, so the resolve's reason "8 would be a leak" does not hold as written; the real trade is a loud load failure for 6- and 7-digit identifiers against a false-refusal rate of about 20 % on very long numeric documents. **DECIDED (lead order 8(2)): the minimum is 6; the second position stays written as the record of the trade and is no longer an open point.**
- **Budget and clock:** the deadline `TEXTNORM_SCAN_BUDGET_S` (proposal 2.0 s) starts at entry of `deny_hit` and is checked after the entity step, after each key and between needles. The clock was `time.thread_time`, the clock `REDACT_BUDGET_S` is measured in (`runmap.py:484`, `started = time.thread_time()`, at `748849c`, re-read). Prototype: one search of the hostile pair (needle `"a"*63+"b"`, haystack `"a"` at the cap) took 1.16 ms, so 500 needles of that form take about 2.3 s per call with 4 searches each, above the 2.0 s budget (the deadline fires and `deny_hit` returns True); random keys took about 0.15 ms. For k lists scanned one after the other the rule was k x (budget + S) <= the caller's allowance; it is NOT in v1 (SK-4 deletes it).
- **Mixed needles (ADV-R4-B10):** a needle that mixes letters and digits (`bank l2345`) is loaded in the id view, where the loose map equates `l` and `1`; in the scan view it is not found in `bank 12345`.
- **The cut rows and mutants:** the 500-needle performance rows, the clock rows, the 10 `needle_problem` rows, the lint rows for `PreparedNeedles(`, `refuse_hidden=False`, `clock=` and `allow_empty=True`, and the mutants of revisions 7 and 8 that guarded them (listed as cut in the Mutation plan arithmetic).
- **Stated limit (4), moved here in round 12 (COR-R12-N-5; history):** a caller that scans for short digit-only values is chance-matched in long numeric text (about 4 % of random 50,000-digit tables and about 20 % of random 250,000-digit tables hit one 6-digit needle). The figures belong to the cut needle API and its digit minimum of 6. P2d-2's rule is a raw value of at least 12 characters with a scan key of at least 8 letters and digits.

## Intake `_f` call sites (documentation only; A14 item 4)
P1 does NOT move Intake's ALLOW check. Intake's `_f` (`invoicecheck.py` lines 465-481) stays in Intake; its invoice-specific steps become `not_taken` corpus rows. `line_view` is cut (revision 10, ADV-R8-6), so there is no generic line step to call; Intake keeps its own line handling until the v1.1 row for `line_view` is started. P1 gives it no offsets (v1.1 row).

## Which view each caller uses, and the stated limits
- **Blocklist and look-alike scan (DENY):** `scan_keyset(text)` per text and per known value, and the caller compares key-sets with substring search (the oracle for the look-alike design is `any(k in t for k in K for t in scan_keyset(T))`, GOLD-1); the contract is SK-1 to SK-6 under "P1b: `scan_keyset`". The caller applies its own minimum length (P2d-2 owns it; it counts after the drop of non-alphanumeric characters) and treats any `TextnormError` as a hit or a refusal, in a function listed in `REFUSAL_HANDLERS`. The many-needle `deny_hit` and the id DENY view are cut to v1.1 (PENDING LEAD 27). The `id_key` equality check of ADV-R5-12 has nothing to refuse because `id_key` is cut; DENY comparisons use `scan_keyset` key-sets.
- **Same person (DENY):** `same_person`. **ALLOW (accept only the verified value):** `allow_equal` only; never a key.
- **Lines of a document:** no view in v1 (`line_view` is cut, PENDING LEAD 32). **Display or storage:** raw text; no key is stored (Interim rule).
- **Stated limits (not hidden):** (1) the scan view over-matches on purpose (`mark` is found in `tomark us` and in `market`; `Anna Maria` equals `Ann Amaria`; `Anna_Schmidt` equals `Anna Schmidt`; `bill` equals `biii`; ADV-R9-1, ADV-R9-2); (2) the pass A/B case limit: a text that mixes capital and small Greek letters inside ONE word may be read one way only (`_scan_match("ΗΗ","hn")` is False); (3) **no decoder runs in any view: HTML entities, JSON escapes, percent escapes and backslash-x escapes are not decoded by P1; they are decoded only by the design's `undo` form read beside the text as written (ADV-R10-1, COR-R10-1)**; (4) (moved to the v1.1 detail, round 12; COR-R12-N-5: the chance-match figures belong to the cut needle API and its digit minimum of 6, not to P1; the later limits keep their numbers); (5) `has_hidden_channel` is TRUE for any tag character, for an England, Scotland or Wales flag emoji (rule 1 refuses the whole tag block), and for a `Variation_Selector` other than U+FE0E and U+FE0F; it is NOT true for a run of zero-width characters, for a run of U+FE0E or U+FE0F (rules 3 and 4 are cut, ADV-R10-3, PENDING LEAD 40), for control, private-use or unassigned characters or for two ANSI colour codes (rules 5 to 7 are cut, ADV-R9-4, PENDING LEAD 35); a caller decides what to do; (6) no cache; (7) the time of one `scan_keyset` call is a stated figure, S_call (SK-4), not a promise; (8) **no limit in this ticket protects a short secret from an encoded copy. These rules bound the COUNTED channels only. Of the channels this signal counts (tags: refused by rule 1; the free selectors: refused by rule 2), nothing gets through. Not counted and UNBOUNDED: every other format, control, private-use, surrogate and unassigned character (dropped by `fold` and the scan keys, but a run of them is not flagged), combining marks (Mn, Me; up to about 359 KB), whitespace characters (up to about 159 KB), U+FE0E and U+FE0F (one bit each; about 32 KB) and characters written as HTML entities (`has_hidden_channel` reads raw text).** **The look-alike check catches a known value of at least 12 characters with a scan key of at least 8 letters and digits (P2d-2's length rule; for stored NAME and ADDRESS values only if precondition P-1 shows at most 1 refusal in 200 benign texts, lead order 13(1)) whose own letters and digits are still in the text, with other characters added between or on them (zero-width or other invisible characters, marks, modifier letters such as the Arabic tatweel, line breaks, spaces, punctuation), or with look-alike letters that the table lists, other widths or other case; a copy written with percent escapes, HTML entities, or JSON or backslash escapes is read through `runmap.undo` (design rows LK-PCT, LK-ENT, LK-ENT-VAL). It does NOT catch, in v1: a shorter known value, or one whose scan key has fewer than 8 letters and digits (for example 12 characters of which 5 are punctuation), written in any way but plainly (look-alike, other-case, marked and split copies of secrets of 8 to 11 characters, which are common passwords, reach the model; the redactor masks every plain copy of a secret kind of 4 or more characters; of a secret kind of exactly 3 characters, and of every other kind, it masks only a plain copy of 3 or more characters that stands alone, is not all digits under 6 and is not a common word); any variant copy of a stored NAME or ADDRESS value if P-1 shows more than 1 refusal in 200 texts; a digit written as a letter where the table has no entry for it (no entry gives `0` or `1`, so ASCII `O` and Cyrillic or Greek `о` or `ο` for `0`, and ASCII `l` for `1`, are not folded; Cyrillic Ze for `3` is); a letter written as a digit (for example `0` for `o`, `1` for `l`, `5` for `s`, `6` for `b`; only `3` for `z` is found, by the class map); a letter or digit the table lacks (for example Cyrillic `и`, and enclosed forms with no compatibility decomposition and no table entry, for example negative circled or negative squared Latin letters, regional-indicator letters, and negative or double circled digits and numbers; row PIN-ENCL selects them by their Unicode names); one copy that needs the capital reading (pass A) at one place and the lower-case reading (pass B) at another, in one word or across words (for example `ΗΗ` against `hn`), or a copy that both replaces a letter by a raised modifier letter and inserts another raised modifier letter (the t view reads the first as missing, the undo view reads the second as a letter; each alone is found); a conjoining Korean jamo placed after a Korean value, or the second part of a two-part vowel sign (category Mc) in 11 scripts (see 'P1b: the DENY keys'); a letter that a font draws as nothing but that is neither one of the seven `FILLERS` nor of a category the fold drops (none is known; FILL-NAMES checks every letter whose Unicode name says FILLER or BLANK); and a known value ENCODED, written as bits or bytes in invisible characters, marks, whitespace or selectors, or in base64 or hex. Neither P1 nor the look-alike check reads such an encoded copy, except an ASCII value written in tag characters, which the tag texts T1 to T3 decode; elsewhere in the core only the tool-output scrubber removes the exact hex and base64 spellings of a secret handed to a tool (`tools.py` `_secret_forms`), and nothing else in v1 (as of `tools.py` `_secret_forms`, `ce70eb5`) is built to catch an encoded copy of a known value.** They are neither refused nor decoded (arithmetic and v1.1 rows under Signals; PENDING LEAD 39); (9) **ASCII digit look-alikes** (ASCII `O` for `0` and ASCII `l` for `1`; COR-R12-N-6) are not supplied by the generated table and are not folded by `fold` or by the scan keys, and neither is a NON-ASCII digit look-alike that the table does not list: no table entry gives `0` or `1`, and Cyrillic `о` (U+043E) and Greek `ο` (U+03BF) give the letter `o`, not `0` (ADV-R11-2; the case-folded sweep for secrets of 8 to 11 characters is the design's v1.1 row LKV-2 for P2d-2, lead order 15, not a P1 item; which non-ASCII look-alikes of the digits the table lacks is UNVERIFIED: the gap-2 digit values come from the round-8 probe); only `squeeze(loose=True)` reads them (relay: Couplings (ASCII look-alikes, ADV-R8-9), in the Relays below); `I`, `l` and `i` are one class in the scan keys since revision 11 (ADV-R9-1); (10) **availability limit (ADV-R8-5):** a `str` subclass gives `NotText`, an over-size text gives `TextTooLong` and one unfoldable stored value makes the check refuse the text; an attacker who controls a stored value can switch a check off in this way if the caller skips it, so P1 never skips and `check_lookalike` refuses (PENDING LEAD 34); (11) **a letter in neither the Unicode data nor the generator's supplement is not folded** (for example Cyrillic `и`; likewise a DIGIT: the dingbat circled digits and the enclosed letters are named in the coverage sentence and pinned by PIN-ENCL, ADV-R13-1; the `748849c` docstring says so, line 28; restored in revision 11, ADV-R9-14; row: `scan_keyset("kra"+U+0138+"key") & scan_keyset("krakkey")` is EMPTY); (12) **a conjoining Korean jamo next to a Korean value joins its syllable under NFKC** (RES-R10-1; a jamo placed after a Korean V changes V's last syllable, and what a reader sees changes too, so such a text is not a copy of V; no step is added, because no order 11 row needs one: a final NFKD would remove it; order 8); (13) **a mixed raised-letter copy is not found (ADV-R13-2, PENDING LEAD 50):** a copy that both replaces a letter by a raised modifier letter (the t view reads it as missing) and inserts another raised modifier letter (the undo view reads it as a letter) matches neither view; each alone is found; pinned by R13-LM-MIX; closing it needs the needle at match time (the v1.1 needle API). Choices a reader can SEE (case, a look-alike letter, a full-width form, a ligature) are not a hidden channel and are out of scope; the look-alike check of the redactor handles copies of KNOWN values.

## Decisions and open points (PENDING LEAD = author proposal written above, lead decides)
**Revision 7 writes every decision out in full (ADV-R5-9).** Revision 6 said "items 2-6 and 8-21 are as in revision 5"; that sentence cannot be followed: revision 5 (commit `b13ff07`, kept only for provenance) had ten decision items, not twenty-one, and a fresh reader does not have it. The list below is complete; the numbers 22 and 23 are the lead's PENDING LEAD numbers and are kept. Correction of a revision 6 remark: it called item 7 "the stored-key salt"; in revision 5 item 7 was "the bible has no rule on folding" and the salt question sat inside the P1c section, which is gone; the salt is moot with P1c. **Revision 8 keeps the numbering, rewrites items 4, 5, 6 and 8, and adds the PENDING LEAD items 24 to 26; lead order 8 decided the digit minimum (6), PENDING LEAD 22 and 23 and the no-cap rule, and order 9 confirmed the P2d cut (L19) and accepted L21 and L22 (their effect on P1 is written under items 6 and 8). Revision 9 keeps the numbering, rewrites items 4, 5, 6 and 8, applies lead order 11 (P2d-2 FOLLOWS P1b; the branches "if L19 stands" are gone) and adds the PENDING LEAD items 27 to 31 (24 narrowed, 25 withdrawn, 26 widened). Revision 11 keeps the numbering, rewrites items 4, 5 and 8, withdraws PENDING LEAD 24 and 28, rewrites 33, narrows 29, widens 26 and adds the PENDING LEAD items 35 to 37.**
1. **Split and tiers.** Three pull requests, P1m, P1a, P1b, in that order; P1c is dropped from v1 (SETTLED by lead order 7(3)). All three are Tier A, P1m included (PENDING LEAD for the plan row: order 6 (b) does not list P1m).
2. **Vendor `confusables.txt`** (763 KB, Unicode License v3) in P1a with the `-text` attribute; fallback in the Files section (PENDING LEAD: size).
3. **Python and the golden test.** The gate is Python 3.13 (Unicode 15.1.0). **The golden test is Test 1 only, with one entry (revision 12, PENDING LEAD 41): the lead no longer adds a base-copy CI step before P1a; the only lead edit left for the golden test is `HADAVIN_REQUIRE_PINNED_UNIDATA=1` on the CI job.** Runs on 3.12 and 3.14 are report-only, never a merge condition; there is no 3.14 probe (v1.1 row).
4. **The DENY keys and the hidden channel (revision 5, changed in revision 7).** (a) two whole-text case readings, a squashed key that holds letters and digits only (revision 11, ADV-R9-2), the class maps `z` to `3` and `l` to `i` in the scan view (ADV-R9-1; the id-view map `3` to `2` is cut with `id_keyset`), four tag texts, at most 8 keys; (b) the spaced reading of memo 1 gap 5 is replaced by the squashed key (a change from the memo); (c) the id-view class is cut to v1.1 (PENDING LEAD 27); (d) pass A and pass B are never mixed inside one text (one consumer reads one way; the row `ΗΗ` against `hn` pins it); (e) the hidden channel: the tag block, the `Variation_Selector` property, **no tiers (revision 11, ADV-R9-4: rules 5 to 7, the two tiers of revisions 8 to 10, `_hidden_tier`, `_HIDDEN_EXCLUDED` and the enumeration test are cut; PENDING LEAD 35)**, **rules 3 and 4 (a selector accepted only after a base, one per base, and the cap) are CUT in revision 12 (ADV-R10-3, PENDING LEAD 40); U+FE0E and U+FE0F are uncounted and unbounded (about 32 KB by symbols at the cap), rule 2 refuses every other `Variation_Selector`**, and the England, Scotland and Wales flags refused as text (stated, ADV-R5-3); (f) `has_bidi_control` and `refuse_bidi` are cut (order 8, PENDING LEAD 26); bidi marks are not counted by any rule; (g) `scan_keyset` has the public contract SK-1 to SK-6 and an AST lint (an allowlist with `REFUSAL_HANDLERS`) bans swallowing handlers and handlers of `TextnormError` outside the listed functions; the many-needle call is cut to v1.1 (PENDING LEAD 27).
5. **Constants (the values, so a reader needs no other file).** `TEXTNORM_MAX_CHARS = MAX_REDACT_CHARS` (262,144; the limit on W(text); the fold may refuse a non-ASCII text the redactor accepts; the option to multiply by `MAX_VALUE_LEN` is dropped); (the needle, minimum and budget constants of revision 8 are CUT with the needle API, PENDING LEAD 27; the digit minimum 6, DECIDED by order 8(2), is a property of the cut design in the v1.1 detail section) `TEXTNORM_PINNED_UNIDATA = "15.1.0"`, and `TEXTNORM_MAX_PASSES = 3` (**three constants in all in revision 12; `TEXTNORM_ENTITY_ROUNDS = 8` is cut with the entity step and `TEXTNORM_MAX_EMOJI_SELECTORS = 512` with rules 3 and 4**; the four hidden-channel constants `TEXTNORM_MAX_INVISIBLE_RUN`, `_MAX_INVISIBLE_TOTAL`, `_MAX_INVISIBLE_PERCENT` and `_MAX_NOPROSE` of revisions 8 to 10 are CUT, ADV-R9-4); no cache (deviates from amendment A2 item 5). All are proposals the builder re-measures; the builder may only LOWER a cap or RAISE a minimum. **Digit minimum (DECIDED by lead order 8(2): 6):** it belongs to the cut needle API; the chance-hit paragraph, the correction of the 8 % figure and the two positions moved whole to the v1.1 detail section. 
6. **Downstream split (lead order 11; ADV-R7-1, COR-R7-1).** Any DENY decision waits for P1b. **P2d-2 FOLLOWS P1b** (it calls `scan_keyset` through `check_lookalike`). **P1's own merge chain is P1m, P1a, P1b; the combined order with the other packages is PENDING LEAD 44 (ADV-R11-1), not a list in this ticket.** P5a depends on P1a and P1b (it maps `TextnormError` by its base class) and on P1m for the runner; P2a depends on P1m for the runner; no package stores a fold key in v1 (Interim rule); P0e answers whether P2a stores names; P3b and P2a state their number D of human decisions per fold change (D = 0 with raw text kept).
7. **The bible has no rule on text folding** (memo 1 section 10; R143, R171 and R354 are the nearest, cited through memo 1 and not re-read by me). A rule "a fold key carries a version" is the lead's to propose; with P1c dropped no key is stored, so the rule has no user in v1.

- **PENDING LEAD 34 (new in revision 10): a stored value that cannot be folded (ADV-R8-5).** Any `TextnormError` raised while folding a stored value means the check REFUSES the text (P1 never skips a check; the contract sentence is under Size rule). An attacker who controls a stored value can raise `TextTooLong` with a stored value over the size rule, so a caller that skipped the value would have its protection switched off on purpose. Recommended: option (a), `check_lookalike` (P2d-2) refuses (counts a hit) when the ORIGINAL cannot be folded, and the design says so under "Values and keys". Alternatives: (b) skip the value and log (rejected: the attacker switches the check off); (c) fold with a fallback reading (new design). **The design agrees (draft 5 item 1, row LK-UNFOLD, PL-LK-28); this relay is closed (ADV-R9-12, CONTESTED by the round-9 resolve: the design already refuses on an unfoldable original).** The design text is not edited here.

Recommended: yes. Confidence: high that the cut costs no order 11 row; medium that no later package wanted the unknown-payload flag (only P2d-2 calls `has_hidden_channel`: round-9 resolve grep; UNVERIFIED by me). Reversible: yes (v1.1 row "a zero-width or control-character run signal").

- **PENDING LEAD 39 (new in revision 12; replaces PENDING LEAD 33): encoded copies of known values are NOT caught in v1 (ADV-R10-2).** Uncounted and unbounded: every format, control, private-use, surrogate and unassigned character, combining marks (about 359 KB at the cap), whitespace (about 159 KB), U+FE0E and U+FE0F (about 32 KB, PENDING LEAD 40) and characters written as HTML entities. The look-alike check catches a known value of at least 12 characters with a scan key of at least 8 letters and digits (P2d-2's length rule; for stored NAME and ADDRESS values only if precondition P-1 shows at most 1 refusal in 200 benign texts, lead order 13(1)) whose own letters and digits are still in the text, with other characters added between or on them (zero-width or other invisible characters, marks, modifier letters such as the Arabic tatweel, line breaks, spaces, punctuation), or with look-alike letters that the table lists, other widths or other case; a copy written with percent escapes, HTML entities, or JSON or backslash escapes is read through `runmap.undo` (design rows LK-PCT, LK-ENT, LK-ENT-VAL). It does NOT catch, in v1: a shorter known value, or one whose scan key has fewer than 8 letters and digits (for example 12 characters of which 5 are punctuation), written in any way but plainly (look-alike, other-case, marked and split copies of secrets of 8 to 11 characters, which are common passwords, reach the model; the redactor masks every plain copy of a secret kind of 4 or more characters; of a secret kind of exactly 3 characters, and of every other kind, it masks only a plain copy of 3 or more characters that stands alone, is not all digits under 6 and is not a common word); any variant copy of a stored NAME or ADDRESS value if P-1 shows more than 1 refusal in 200 texts; a digit written as a letter where the table has no entry for it (no entry gives `0` or `1`, so ASCII `O` and Cyrillic or Greek `о` or `ο` for `0`, and ASCII `l` for `1`, are not folded; Cyrillic Ze for `3` is); a letter written as a digit (for example `0` for `o`, `1` for `l`, `5` for `s`, `6` for `b`; only `3` for `z` is found, by the class map); a letter or digit the table lacks (for example Cyrillic `и`, and enclosed forms with no compatibility decomposition and no table entry, for example negative circled or negative squared Latin letters, regional-indicator letters, and negative or double circled digits and numbers; row PIN-ENCL selects them by their Unicode names); one copy that needs the capital reading (pass A) at one place and the lower-case reading (pass B) at another, in one word or across words (for example `ΗΗ` against `hn`), or a copy that both replaces a letter by a raised modifier letter and inserts another raised modifier letter (the t view reads the first as missing, the undo view reads the second as a letter; each alone is found); a conjoining Korean jamo placed after a Korean value, or the second part of a two-part vowel sign (category Mc) in 11 scripts (see 'P1b: the DENY keys'); a letter that a font draws as nothing but that is neither one of the seven `FILLERS` nor of a category the fold drops (none is known; FILL-NAMES checks every letter whose Unicode name says FILLER or BLANK); and a known value ENCODED, written as bits or bytes in invisible characters, marks, whitespace or selectors, or in base64 or hex. Neither P1 nor the look-alike check reads such an encoded copy, except an ASCII value written in tag characters, which the tag texts T1 to T3 decode; elsewhere in the core only the tool-output scrubber removes the exact hex and base64 spellings of a secret handed to a tool (`tools.py` `_secret_forms`), and nothing else in v1 (as of `tools.py` `_secret_forms`, `ce70eb5`) is built to catch an encoded copy of a known value. **DECIDED by lead orders 14(1), 14(2) and 15 (COR-R11-1): accepted with the coverage sentence above (the secrets of 8 to 11 characters are "only plain copies", order 15), and the v1.1 row "an encoded-copy check for known values" has the lead's trigger "before any pilot with real client data" (COR-R11-2).** **Revision 14 (ADV-R12-M2): the sentence above is the NARROWER TRUE text, not the lead's words of order 14(1) ("caught by nothing in v1"), which are untrue for the exact hex and base64 spellings of a secret handed to a tool; PENDING LEAD 46 asks the lead to confirm it. Modifier letters (Lm) are now on the "catches" side (PENDING LEAD 47).** **Revision 15 (round 13): the sentence also names the enclosed and dingbat forms, the mixed raised-letter copy, the 4-character redactor floor, the Mc join and the `_secret_forms` date (PENDING LEAD 49 and 50).** Confidence: high (the scan keys drop exactly those characters). Reversible: yes. The design decides base64 for v1 (relay below).

- **PENDING LEAD 49 (new in revision 15): enclosed and dingbat forms (ADV-R13-1).** The negative circled and squared letters (U+1F150-1F189), the regional-indicator letters (U+1F1E6-1F1FF) and the dingbat circled digits (U+2776-2793, U+24F5-24FF) have no compatibility decomposition and no table entry; a copy written with them matches no key and reaches the model (probed by the round-13 resolve on the stand-in; not re-run by me). Recommendation (final under orders 6(g) and 9 unless the lead replies): name them as not caught in v1 (the coverage sentence), pin them with PIN-ENCL, and add the v1.1 row with the trigger 'before any pilot with real client data'. Alternative: add the entries now (about 100 table rows; the table digest, the golden file and the counts change). Confidence: 70 percent. Reversible: yes. - **PENDING LEAD 50 (new in revision 15): the mixed raised-letter copy (ADV-R13-2).** A copy that both replaces a letter by a raised modifier letter and inserts another raised modifier letter reaches the model in v1; each alone is caught. Closing it needs the needle at match time (the v1.1 needle API); a third reading would double the keys to 16 and still miss it. Recommendation: state it (the coverage sentence), pin it (R13-LM-MIX), add no third reading. Confidence: 85 percent. Reversible: yes. **CLOSED in revision 16: CONFIRMED by lead order 16(1) (state and pin the limit).** Answered by the lead: YES, order 16(1).

Recommendation: yes. Confidence: 80 percent. Reversible: yes. **CLOSED in revision 16: CONFIRMED by lead order 16(1) (add U+13441 and U+13442 to `FILLERS`); the row FILL-NAMES is narrowed to name matches that are letters or digits (ADV-R14-1).** Answered by the lead: YES, order 16(1).

- **PENDING LEAD 52 (new in revision 16): the table-digest check moves from import time to a test (ADV-R14-2).** What: delete the import-time `TABLE_DIGEST` comparison in `textnorm.py`; add `test_the_checked_in_table_matches_its_digest` (never skips); cut the P1a mutant "the table digest check removed at import"; give mutants 7, 8, 9 and 18 `only_test=`. Options: (a) the four mutants patch the digest too (rejected: a search string that spans the table from the entry to the digest line, rebuilt whenever P1b regenerates the table, and a computed digest in the mutants file: new surface on the mutation path for no new protection); (b) `allow_crash` plus a pinned import test (rejected with a run by the round-14 resolve: the killers import `textnorm` at the top of their file, so pytest exits with code 2, which the judge reports as ERROR, and `allow_crash` cannot make an ERROR good); (c) this. Recommendation: (c), yes. Cost: an installed or vendored copy hand-edited outside CI is no longer refused at import (the core-sync check's job; no defence against an attacker either way). Confidence: 80 percent. Reversible: yes (the check can return at import if the four mutants are given `allow_crash` and lazy-import killers). Money: none. Final under orders 6(g), 9 and 16 unless the lead replies. Answered by the lead: no.

The evidence is `reviews/rung-audit-1-part-F.md`, section "P1m: rung table" and its answer to order 56 item 4: mutmut 3.8.1 and cosmic-ray 8.7.0 were run on 2026-11-06 on a 3-test sample, both counted a `TypeError` crash as "killed", and neither takes a named hand-written mutant, while the kill-reason judge (`testing/mutation.py`, 107 lines) is exactly what they lack. Lesson: the rung was never tried before P1m was built (the audit greps found 0 mentions of mutmut or cosmic-ray in the ticket or reviews), so the next package that needs a runner or judge starts with a tool check; the audit also says the 80 percent figure is not proven either way and no real P1m mutant was run through either tool (part F, open items 5 and 6). I re-ran nothing. T-092 now has this evidence in the audit file, not here.
  - **`_confusables` table.** Verdict KEEP-CODE (already generated from the pinned data; no code change here). Record of the pin as found (T-118): data file `tools/data/confusables.txt`, Unicode UTS #39 confusables data, version 18.0.0, file dated 2026-08-06 01:05:35 GMT, 9,862 lines, 763,128 bytes, sha256 `6ed3ee967c9dfdf6677d563c9985182fbc50a2efb7d6059cd57b2e2ce18f5b92` (I recomputed it on a P1m mutant snapshot copy of the tree; the ticket and part F give the same value). The generator `tools/gen_confusables.py` writes `_confusables.py` with `DATA_VERSION = "18.0.0"`, `DATA_SHA256` as above, `ENTRIES = 677`, `TABLE_DIGEST = 1dce77c4d2d9e406d9b0e71749e459b86ae4adfbae35f30c1a8654118abf19c3`, and `DATA_UNICODEDATA = "15.1.0"` (the small-capital names come from the interpreter). The source of the table is the Unicode data through the generator, so there is nothing to cut. One thing to watch, not a defect found: the table data is Unicode 18.0.0 while the interpreter pin is 15.1.0; a code point assigned after 15.1.0 is mapped by the table but unknown to `unicodedata` (not tested; UNVERIFIED). P1b's generated script data (BN-14) must carry its own dated file and version in the same way.
  - **`FOLD_VERSION`, `check_pinned_unidata` (`require_pinned_unidata`), `TABLE_DIGEST`.** Verdict KEEP-CODE. Guarantee, as the lead worded it: **the same text folds the same way on every run and every Python, or the start refuses.** Scope as built (BN-12, `defaults.py` pin `TEXTNORM_PINNED_UNIDATA = "15.1.0"`): the refusal applies in pilot and prod; in dev and test another Unicode version is allowed and the key's alg tag then names that version, so two Pythons never produce equal keys. This answers the rung table's question "which v1 guarantee needs it": the DENY and identity comparison (`same_person`, `allow_equal`) compares keys built at different times, so a silent change of fold would turn an old DENY into an ALLOW. Evidence for the pin being needed: part F ran `unicodedata2` 18.0.0 and found it lacks `casefold`, `isalpha`, `isspace`, `isdecimal`, so a library cannot pin the whole fold (part F, row "Pinned Unicode version"; T-118 for the pin values). No tool replaces it.
  - **Refusal lint (AST test: a DENY caller treats `TextnormError` as REFUSE).** Verdict KEEP-CODE. Checked (T-113, T-114) with ruff 0.17.4 and semgrep 1.184.0 in a temp venv on a fixture of five shapes (`except TextnormError`, `except textnorm.NotText`, a tuple with an aliased name, `contextlib.suppress(TextnormError)`, `except Exception`) plus an allowed handler inside `check_lookalike`. ruff `TID251` (banned-api) flagged the import line of the banned names and the dotted use `textnorm.NotText`; it never looks at a handler, so the plain `except TextnormError`, the tuple and the `suppress` rows were reported only through their import line (a handler that gets the name some other way is not seen); its exemption is per file (`per-file-ignores`) or per line (`noqa`), not per enclosing function, so the `REFUSAL_HANDLERS` allowlist cannot be written; `BLE001` flagged only `except Exception`. No ruff rule bans handlers of a named exception around a call of a named module. A first semgrep rule (try/except with a name regex, `pattern-not-inside` the allowed function) caught 2 of the 4 swallow shapes (the plain name and the dotted name) and missed the tuple-with-alias and the `suppress` shapes; with more rules it may reach more, so it is not proven to fail at 80 percent either way, but it is far from the 9-row fixture list as written. The single property both tools fail with: the function-level allowlist plus the alias and tuple shapes (reproduction above; fixture is the ticket's own nine rows). The AST test stays, and the builder of P1b should try one semgrep rule for rows (1) and (2) only if it saves the lint more than it costs; that is not a cut.

- **BN-16 (lead order 61 item 5; for the P1b build; redaction/identity path, review UNCAPPED; this note wins over BN-15 on the pin).** The table data must match the interpreter pin. Use the UTS #39 `confusables.txt` of the SAME Unicode version as the interpreter pin (15.1.0, archived under unicode.org/Public/security/), dated and hashed in the generator. Unicode 18 data against a 15.1.0 interpreter is a mismatch and must not ship; the generator refuses a data file whose version differs from the pin, and a test checks it. Counts not recomputed (UNVERIFIED).

- **BN-17 (P1a fix2 outcome, relayed by the lead's worker; for the P1b build; supersedes the BN-12 wording "fold1 reads as fold2").** The fold algorithm id is now **fold3** (P1a fix2, head `c735846` on `core/P1a`): U+0345 becomes an iota only after a Greek letter (dropped characters in between are skipped) and is removed elsewhere; the only key difference from `4df0ccd` over all 1,112,064 code points and the 171-row corpus is U+0345. Text that names fold1 or fold2 reads as fold3. `require_pinned_unidata()` is called by nothing in P1a: a caller that does not call it (any dev or test caller on Python 3.12, Unicode 15.0.0) gets tagged keys (U+E000 + `alg=fold3|ud=15.0.0` + U+E000 + key) that never equal a key made on the pinned version. A raw text that starts with the interpreter's own tag has the tag stripped and gets the same key as the text without it (an over-collision, the safe side of a DENY; tested). The `has_mixed_script` gap for Runic, Tamil and similar scripts is BN-14, owned by P1b. UNVERIFIED: the U+0345 rule is a redaction/identity-path choice that the lead must confirm.

- **BN-18 (lead order 65, P1a round 3 NOT CLEAN; for the P1b build; redaction/identity path, review UNCAPPED).** P1a fix3 (PR 63) adds `identity_keys(raw)` and `fold_views(text)` returning a small fixed set of readings (always-drop U+0345; iota rule; upper-side table-first reading for Greek capitals Eta, Mu, Nu, Upsilon); `same_person` and `allow_equal` are true when ANY reading matches; `identity_key` stays one reading and is NOT a DENY comparator; `TEXTNORM_MAX_PASSES` is removed from P1a and P1b adds it with its reader. P1b RECORDS these sibling paths (not done in fix3), each with a test that every redaction view equals fold's view on the look-alike and mark corpus: `runmap.canonical` / `_canonical_view` and `evidence.py` grounding (keeps Mn, fillers, private use, no look-alike fold); `guard/injection.py` (Cyrillic o in "ignore previous instructions" scores 0); `guard/egress.py` (IDNA 2003 vs `allow_equal`; state whether egress is a consumer; ledger p1a-011); the policy for characters unassigned on the pinned interpreter (Unicode 16+ look-alikes are dropped; decide drop vs mark, with a hostile test; the "dead table entry" claim did NOT reproduce, 0 Cn sources); `app.build` must refuse an unpinned interpreter; a 3.14 non-blocking job when the pin moves. UNVERIFIED until the fix3 head is read.

- **BN-19 (P1b merge with P1a fix3 outcome, 2026-11-06; append-only; corrects two hand traces in this ticket; redaction/identity path, review UNCAPPED).** The P1b builder merged fix3 (68efd66) into its branch locally (head 1c12470, not pushed). Hand traces re-checked against the merged code: `identity_key` is reading B (case-fold first, then the iota rule, then the table), not the table-first reading A; U+04CF gives `identity_keys` (`l`,), `identity_key` `l` and scan key `i` (override `l`, then the class map `l` to `i`), so "U+04CF reads i in the keys" is true only of the scan key; capital Eta gives (`n`,`h`); "ᾳdmin" and "admin": `could_be_same_person` (then called `same_person`) is true; INV + U+2212 + 2026 and INV-2026: true (corpus rows r114-r117 now resolve). The merge renamed the algorithm id to `fold5` (it collides with fold4 but gives other keys), re-added `TEXTNORM_MAX_PASSES` and `FoldNotConverged` for the scan loop, and replaced the 18.0.0-filtered table by the 15.1.0 table (507 entries). Consequence of P1a fix4 (order 79): P1b must be merged again on the fix4 head (15.1.0 data comes from P1a, rename `same_person` to `could_be_same_person`, redaction scans BOTH `fold_views` and `squeeze_views`, an empty view is refused). Runner finding: a scan route made `same_person` blind to a missing reading, so six mutants survived or died for the wrong reason; tests must also call `readings_match` (identity_keys only). Sibling gaps pinned by tests, not fixed: `runmap.canonical` vs `_canonical_view` across combining marks; the injection `RegexDetector` misses a Cyrillic o; `evidence.normalise` keeps marks and look-alikes; a look-alike placeholder marker is not recognised; egress refuses a look-alike host. UNVERIFIED by me: the builder's counts (full suite 10321 passed, 1 failed before the last test fix, 454 skipped; 87 of 87 mutants good); the full suite was not re-run after the last test-only edits (692 text-normalisation tests passed on 3.13 and 3.12).

- **BN-20 (P1a fix4 erratum, 2026-11-06; append-only; redaction/identity path). BN-20 wins over every older name in this ticket.** The names `identity_keys`, `fold_views`, `squeeze_views`, `key_tag` and `require_pinned_unidata` of P1a (PR 63, fix4) SUPERSEDE `scan_keyset` wherever this ticket uses it; `fold_key` is deleted (use `identity_key`); `same_person` is now `could_be_same_person`, already built on all spelling variants in P1a (not widened in P1b); `check_pinned_unidata(strict=...)` is replaced by `require_pinned_unidata()` which only raises `UnidataMismatch`. The P1b scope is: gaps 1-5 (gap 7, both halves, cut to v1.1), the DENY scans using the P1a views (`fold_views` AND `squeeze_views`, both on every scan; an empty view is refused), and `require_pinned_unidata()` called in `app.build`. The 20 public names of `hadavin_sdk.guard.textnorm` and the full text are in `reviews/p1a-fix4/erratum.md`; P1b notes in `reviews/p1a-fix4/P1b-notes.md`.
